campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/castle-ward.md:27: - **Services:** Magister courts and courthouse cells. City Guard barracks. Palace exchequers who exchange coin without a fee. The Code Legal, kept at the Palace in several languages. The Font of Knowledge, the city's largest public library, with free reading lessons. Public privies around the Market. The Map House, guildhall of the Surveyors', Map- and Chart-makers' Guild.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/castle-ward.md:34: - **Economy:** The Market, solicitors, publishers and business offices, courts, and the Palace coin exchange. Guild law applies as everywhere in the city.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/castle-ward.md:94: > - Keln keeps the Terasse outpost for Xanathar's Guild. He holds two passphrases: one opens the clinic's gladiator transport route, and the other opens the route to the Lair.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/castle-ward.md:162: - Oghma's priests at the Font of Knowledge teach reading free. The Dungsweepers' Guild keeps the streets clean at every hour, and the Market's public privies serve the crowds.
campaign/gazetteer/waterdeep/castle-ward.md:247: - **Xanathar's Guild**: the guild behind Terasse Estate and the stair.

campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:61: | 25 | **Wise Owl** | Mirt arranges a private audience with Laeral Silverhand if the matter is urgent. Harpers provide cover for one sensitive operation per quest: forged documents, coordinated distractions, or witnesses who saw nothing. Harper informants embedded in Xanathar's Guild, Sea Maidens Faire, and the Splinter can be activated for one specific intelligence request each per quest (DC 13 Charisma check; failure means the informant is unavailable for two tendays). A second *persona* is learned. When any villain faction designates a character as a priority target, Mirt's network detects it and passes warning within 24 hours. |

campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:68: | 10 | **Stingblade** | Once per quest, Jalester arranges an introduction to one of eight named Waterdhavians: Magistrate Barnibus Blastwind, Lord Torlyn Wands, Saeth Cromley, Valetta, Factor Tessaly Brightwater, Guildmaster Piers Hobb, Clerk Maelith Dray or Lady Perrine Ostler. Once per quest, the Alliance's solicitor pays up to 500 gp of bail, fines and damages for one genuine incident. The member reads the Watch's files at the central records office. Standard files take one hour, and classified files take four hours and a **DC 12 Charisma (Persuasion)** check with the file officer. |

campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:93: **Fentree's Ledger** ends in one of three outcomes. **Ledger Delivered** is the main trigger for the audience and the evidence for **Escalation to the Open Lord**. **Ledger Sold to the Guild** and **Ledger Sold to the Cassalanters** are read by **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa** and **Vault of Dragons**, whichever has not yet run. If the ledger was sold, the summons to the audience comes at Renown 13 and 7th level after **Kolat Towers**, whether or not **Escalation to the Open Lord** has run.

campaign/guides/factions/04-emerald-enclave.md:76: | Renown 8, 5th level | **The Grells in the Dock Ward** | Guild excavation under the south quay has disturbed two grells, and three people have vanished from Pier 17. Find the nest and bring out the third victim alive. | +3 |
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:31: | **Xanathar's Lair** | The Guild's use of intellect devourers is an abomination in Savra's theological framework. She asks the party to put Nihiloor down for good. Nihiloor always escapes, so she asks for the attempt and not the kill. A Whitehawk member also gets her lair support: she waits across from the guildsign entrance and fights beside the party if called, which spends the quest's one Marcheon operation. |
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:37: Savra knocks once at Trollskull Manor and waits to be asked in. If nobody is home, she leaves a note with Tally Fellbranch. She names two targets: a beholder said to run the Guild beneath the city, and a Sea Ward family she will not name. She states the cost of the work plainly, including a quarter of what a member earns on big jobs. A character who asks sincerely about her past learns she spent three years in the Howling Hatred cult, and nothing about what she did there.
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:39: Every character who belongs to no other faction is a candidate and answers for themself: accept, decline or ask for time. A declined offer stays open, and the Event writes no closing outcome. Candidates who accept swear the oath at the Halls of Justice in the Castle Ward, which is open every day. A priest of Tyr seals it over a silver gauntlet, and the member becomes a Chevall at Renown 1 with the benefits below. Savra walks the party to a Guild hall in Order insignia when the first member swears, which cuts the renovation total of Trollskull Manor by 100 gp once. **Breaking Up the Brawl** reaches each member at the manor the next morning, once the member is 2nd level.
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:76: | Renown 1, 2nd level | [**Breaking Up the Brawl**](../../quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/m01-breaking-up-the-brawl/overview.md) | Stop four hired men from cornering a Xanathar's Guild dealer in a Field Ward square at noon. The man who paid them works for the Splinter, and the party can learn what he looks like. | +2 |
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:9: She learns the rest incrementally through the party. Mission 1 adds Hlam's warning about the buried thing waking. Mission 2 brings a report of a vessel fixed under the *Eyecatcher*'s hull, which is the Bregan D'aerthe submersible, and she never learns whose it is. By Mission 4 she knows where Nihiloor's pool lies, from one report by an agent inside the Guild who has not answered since. When the party delivers the full Grand Game picture, with all four factions, the Stone, the vault and the timeline, she goes still for a long time and then begins writing.

Vajra wants a full debrief. Specifically: any sign of Nihiloor's presence, any implanted citizens encountered, any evidence of coordination between the Guild and outside parties. |

campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:9: Rather than competing with Xanathar or Manshoon for the Stone, Jarlaxle already holds Eye #3, aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth* and mounted beneath the *Eyecatcher*'s keel, and is content to let the other factions exhaust themselves chasing each other. His asset inside Xanathar's organization — **Nar'l Xibrindas**, a drow mage who serves as the beholder's advisor and reports to Jarlaxle by *Sending* spell — keeps him informed of the Guild's movements. The Sea Maidens Faire parades give him cover to move personnel and materials through any ward without scrutiny.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:47: - **Protect Nar'l Xibrindas's cover within Xanathar's Guild (+1).** Any action that deflects suspicion from Nar'l, discredits a threat to his position, or removes someone who has come close to identifying him earns D'aerthe renown — whether or not Jarlaxle explicitly requested it.

Three approaches: feed him a false report, extract him from the city, or confront Ahmaergo's counterintelligence source. | +3 |

campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:1: # Xanathar's Guild
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:3: Game mechanics for running Xanathar's Guild as a villain faction. For lore, operations, and key members, see the **Xanathar's Guild** organization page.
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:21: A modest rowhouse secretly owned by Xanathar, the Terasse Estate operates on two levels — both under the same roof, both accessible by passphrase, and neither officially connected. Keln Trigos runs a healing operation for Guild agents on the upper floors. Below it, the estate serves as one of Waterdeep's gladiatorial gathering points: those who want to attend the fights in Xanathar's Lair report here; staff ply them with exotic food, then blindfold them and escort them through an underground dock to caverns near Skullport.
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:31: **Areas 8–10 — Sick Rooms.** A bedridden Guild agent has an *Ill Man's Note* on his bedside table: the Orb Confectioners passphrase, written in verse.
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:33: ***Roster.*** Keln Trigos (**priest**) + 2 **guards** + 3 Guild agents (**thug** stat block).
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:39: *Independent discovery path: any character asking about Xanathar weapons, black-market arms, or Guild suppliers in the Dock Ward is pointed to Grinda in Mistshore within a day (DC 12 Charisma check, no faction membership required). This is the Xanathar chain's street-level entry point.*
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:41: Grinda runs her operation out of the standard Mistshore description, ignoring seasonal variants. She is Xanathar's black market armorer, equipping Guild agents on request and freelancing to third parties for coin.
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:107: - **Xanathar's Guild** organization page: operations, key members, overview
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:25: *Backroom.* A rack and other instruments of torture. An arcane circle on the floor. Strapped into the rack is **Ott Steeltoes**, a Xanathar's Guild agent captured while the Zhentarim were trying to locate the Eye. He knows nothing of the Eye itself.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:9: **All four villain factions run simultaneously.** No season villain. Xanathar's Guild, Manshoon's Zhentarim, the Cassalanters, and Bregan D'aerthe are active from session one, each pursuing a different angle on the same prize.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:104: The job was supposed to be clean. Xanathar's Guild was acquiring something from a rival organization, and you were hired to facilitate transit and paid well enough not to ask questions. What came out of there wasn't what you'd been told. What you left behind was considerably worse. The Guild got its item. The irregularities were absorbed into a larger accounting of that night, and your involvement went officially unnoted.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:106: Ahmaergo, Xanathar's dwarven majordomo and the man who actually runs the Guild's logistics, keeps a private ledger separate from the official records. Your name is in it, under a column that reads *Outstanding*.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:108: *Your character knows the following:* The Guild doesn't know, officially, that you exist. Ahmaergo knows. He made one indirect inquiry months after the event, to confirm you were still in Waterdeep and still capable. You confirmed both by answering.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:110: ### The Holder: Ahmaergo (Xanathar's Guild)
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:112: Ahmaergo is the majordomo of Xanathar's Guild: a shield dwarf of outward civility and exacting administrative competence who functions as the Guild's operational second-in-command. Most orders that bear Xanathar's name were drafted by Ahmaergo. His private ledger is separate from official Guild records because Xanathar doesn't know about every arrangement he makes, which is precisely the point.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:114: His first formal contact arrives as a logistics problem requiring someone unaffiliated, deniable, and capable. He won't frame it as debt repayment. Characters who complete the task find the *Outstanding* column now reads *Current*. Characters who refuse find their name appearing in official Guild records, in a context that requires immediate and uncomfortable explanation to the City Watch.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:117: > Ahmaergo keeps his private ledger separate from Guild records because Xanathar doesn't know about every arrangement he runs. If Xanathar becomes aware of the character's existence — through carelessness, another character's indiscretion, or any direct contact with the Guild — the dynamic changes entirely. Xanathar doesn't distinguish between asset, debtor, and enemy when its paranoia is engaged. Hold the beholder back from this relationship for as long as dramatically possible.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:155: Skeemo Weirdbottle maintains, out of a cramped shop on a side street in the Trades Ward, one of the most comprehensive indices of Waterdeep's criminal and semi-criminal activities that exist outside Xanathar's Guild's own records. He compiled it partly for business and partly because he finds the city's power structures intrinsically interesting. He sold you something from it (access, a specific piece of information, a document that enabled something you needed to do) and conducted the transaction with professional discretion.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:61: Manshoon's Zhentarim and Xanathar's Guild are in active conflict at street level in the Dock Ward. Manshoon's operatives probe Guild territory regularly; Xanathar has issued a kill order on Manshoon that has failed twice. This is a grinding low-level engagement that produces casualties on both sides and keeps both factions consuming resources on a front entirely separate from the PCs.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:72: > This is the Grand Game's self-reinforcing pressure. If the PCs dismantle Xanathar's Guild outposts, Manshoon's Zhentarim moves into the vacated Dock Ward territory. If the PCs weaken Manshoon, Xanathar's paranoia briefly lifts and it turns outward. The PCs' choices produce real chain reactions because thinking factions respond to changing conditions. The inter-faction operations matrix in **Running the Villains** shows where each faction's active operations overlap and which documents cross faction lines; consult it whenever the party clears territory a rival faction would want.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:101: - [Xanathar's Guild](../factions/09-xanathars-guild.md) — response teams, revelation list
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:163: | Doom Raiders | **Davil Starsong** (Missions 1–2); **Tashlyn Yafeera** (Missions 3–6) | Intelligence on Manshoon's cell; containment of Xanathar's Guild activity; anything that weakens their rivals |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:200: The seven factions above are the organizations available for player characters to join. The other major powers active in Waterdeep during the Grand Game — **Xanathar's Guild**, **Manshoon's Zhentarim splinter**, and the **Cassalanters** — are adversary factions. Players may interact with, negotiate with, or broker deals with these organizations, but formal membership is not available through normal play. See **Notable Figures** for details on their leaders and agents, and **Running the Villains** (GM Guide) for how to run them at the table.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:25: **Eye #3** — held by Jarlaxle Baenre, aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*. It began in Renaer Neverember's mourning locket. Manshoon's Zhentarim seized the locket the night before Ches 1 and pried it open at the Candle Lane warehouse, Xanathar's Guild raided the warehouse and carried off the Eye with the rest of the back office without knowing what it was, and Bregan D'aerthe agents already inside Xanathar's hideout lifted it the same night. It reached Jarlaxle before the party reached the hideout, and Manshoon's cell never held it. Jarlaxle has known of the Stone, the Eyes and the vault since he arrived in Waterdeep. He is active from Act I through his personas — J.B. Nevercott in Trollskull Alley and Zardoz Zord at the Field of Triumph — and his villain escalation keys off the **Jarlaxle Informed** flag set during **Fireball!**, which records only that the party made contact with him.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:30: > **Eye #3:** Jarlaxle Baenre — aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*. Jarlaxle knows exactly what it is. It passed from Renaer's locket to the Zhentarim, then to Xanathar's Guild in the warehouse raid, and Bregan D'aerthe agents inside Xanathar's hideout lifted it that night. Manshoon holds no Eye.

campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/02-operating-costs.md:5: Three expense categories recur every tenday. Guild fees and staff wages together form the **base 60 gp/tenday** figure established during Act I. Supply costs sit on top and scale with Fame.

campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/02-operating-costs.md:59: **GM guidance:** Don't track supply costs to the gold piece every session. Use them as a narrative lever — two tendays away adventuring, the cook ran supplies low, 40 gp owed to the Vintners' Guild. The number matters most when the room stops filling from faction pressure.

campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/02-operating-costs.md:83: - **Order of the Gauntlet (Savra Belabranta):** Savra walks the party to one Guild hall in Order insignia, and the permit queue closes within a day. Renovation cost drops by 100 gp, once, when the first member swears the oath. A Watch patrol also answers any disturbance at the manor within the hour, until a Watch citation against the manor ends it for a tenday.
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/02-operating-costs.md:84: - **Doom Raiders (Davil + Istrid Horn):** Istrid: loan up to 400 gp, no interest, party-set repayment schedule. Davil: Black Network bulk agreements reduce opening stock cost ~100 gp through Vintners' Guild bypass.

The GM should track when this shift occurs and let the party notice gradually — the tavern is where early warning signs should emerge. The Force Grey mission "The Trouble with Meloon" formalizes this.

We want it back. Or we want something worth the same. You have until the new moon." Then leaves. His team is outside.
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/09-response-teams-at-the-tavern.md:30: **What the party can do:** Negotiate, return goods (if goods rather than information), or prepare for Korgstrod's team. No social check makes the Guild forget what they lost. Exchange or escalation.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/10-false-vault.md:21: **The Deception.** Guild blueprints from Scene 2 (Doom Raider contact) show this room's dimensions, which are measurably smaller than the basement footprint implies — the west wall is four feet farther in on the plans than the actual basement perimeter. This is the mechanical clue that C27 is a decoy. A party with the blueprints can identify this discrepancy with a DC 13 Intelligence (Investigation) check comparing the plans to their observations. The real continuation is through C28 (wine cellar).
campaign/locations/trollskull-manor/tm02-kitchen.md:5: **Renovation state.** The kitchen is the most labor-intensive renovation target outside the taproom — grime, broken equipment, and an oven that needs relining before it can be used safely. The Carpenters' Guild work includes the kitchen shelving. The Bakers' Guild inspector checks the oven. Embric, who critiqued the chimney flashing in the courtyard, is also available for the kitchen hearth — his assessment ("it was always wrong") applies here too.
campaign/locations/trollskull-manor/tm03-cellar.md:5: **The sewer grate (T7).** At the east end of the cellar, a heavy iron grate set into the floor opens onto city sewer infrastructure. The Cellarers' and Plumbers' Guild inspector identified this connection during the ev-02 permit inspection and flagged it for "structural heritage documentation." The infrastructure predates the current building by at least two centuries.
campaign/locations/trollskull-manor/tm03-cellar.md:13: **Storage function.** After opening, the cellar holds tavern stock: wine casks, ale casks (Hammond Kraddoc's guild-recommended selections in pride of place), preserved goods from the Guild of Butchers and Bakers' Guild suppliers.
campaign/locations/trollskull-manor/tm04-upper-floors.md:5: **Renovation state.** Empty rooms being cleaned and assessed. The Carpenters' Guild work includes these floors — window frames, door hardware, floorboard stabilization. Some rooms have balconies; the balcony hardware is checked by the Roofers' Guild.
campaign/locations/trollskull-manor/tm05-private-rooms.md:5: **Renovation state.** Habitable, if sparsely. The party can use these rooms as living quarters from the day they arrive; they are not part of the active renovation work. The Carpenters' Guild checks the window frames; the rest is cosmetic. Lif ignores this floor almost entirely during the escalation — this is the party's space and he respects the distinction.

campaign/locations/zhentarim-warehouse/area-overview.md:53: The twelve corpses in Z01 are the remnants of two organizations that met violently. Five Zhentarim thugs (flying snake tattoos on neck or forearm) and seven Xanathar's Guild thugs, one of whom has Xanathar's circle-and-spokes mark tattooed on their right palm. Rapiers and daggers lie nearby. The fight was no more than a day old.
campaign/locations/zhentarim-warehouse/z02-storage-closet.md:17: - Xanathar's Guild attacked before the interrogation went further; he slipped his bonds in the confusion and hid here
campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/design-notes.md:9: ***The Two Zhentarims in Finding Floon.*** Dragon Heist presents the Zhentarim as a single faction. This remix uses Finding Floon to establish the faction fracture that defines the Manshoon subplot. Yagra Stonefist (a Doom Raiders operative, Davil Starsong's people) is at the Yawning Portal bar, openly wearing the Zhentarim pendant, fighting Xanathar's Guild thugs. The Candle Lane warehouse belongs to Manshoon's cell, Urstul Floxin's people, operating separately from Davil. Players who later join the Doom Raiders in Trollskull Alley and then raid Kolat Towers in Kolat Towers will have seen both Zhentarim factions from Scene 1: one as a potential ally, one as an adversary, both operating under the same symbol. Players feel the distinction before anyone explains it.
campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/design-notes.md:17: ***Nihiloor as Foreshadowing.*** Q07 in the sewer hideout is the campaign's first contact with Nihiloor. Xanathar's Lair eventually identifies it as the true intelligence driving Xanathar's Guild, running three parallel projects, exercising psionic control over Xanathar, and infiltrating Waterdeep's civil institutions (see the **Xanathar's Guild** organization page). In Finding Floon, Nihiloor doesn't fight them. It's simply there — seated, watching Grum'shar work — and then it's gone, faster than a creature that large has any right to move, through a portal they can't follow. This restraint is intentional; the encounter registers the creature's existence and its agency over the Guild cell before any context is available to understand what it is. Nihiloor's full scope surfaces across Trollskull Alley through Xanathar's Lair. By the time they reach Undermountain Level 17 in the Dungeon of the Mad Mage, its presence in Finding Floon should read as a thread that began in the sewers under Trollskull Alley.
campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/design-notes.md:21: ***Renaer's Introduction.*** In the source, finding Renaer in the warehouse is a pleasant surprise in an otherwise thin scene (a friendly NPC who points the party downward). This treatment makes Renaer's introduction structural: the warehouse is the quest's pivot. The party arrives expecting Floon and finds someone who matters more to the campaign's back story. Renaer's knowledge state is asymmetric by design. He knows the Zhentarim took his locket and that the Guild attacked before they finished with him, without knowing what the locket contained or where it went. His ignorance is genuine. This positions him as a trustworthy ally (he doesn't conceal what he knows) while preserving the campaign's central mystery (the nature of the Eyes, the Vault's location). The gap between what they'll eventually learn and what Renaer knows gives the warehouse scene a retrospective weight absent from the source text.
campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/ev-01-yawning-portal.md:10: > - A bar brawl erupts between Yagra Stonefist (Doom Raiders) and Krentz's Xanathar's Guild crew

She has been nursing a drink while watching a table of five Xanathar's Guild thugs, led by **Krentz**, a man with eye-shaped tattoos on his shaved scalp. Two nights ago, Krentz's crew killed a Doom Raiders operative in the Dock Ward. When one of Krentz's companions says something audible about snake men getting what they deserved, Yagra moves.

The Guild's raid then carried the Eye out of the back office without knowing what it was, and the party can follow its trail in the **Xanathar Sewer Hideout** and **The Eyes of the Stone**.

The man they found was Renaer Neverember, the former Open Lord's estranged son, grabbed by mistake and hidden in a back room when the raid hit. Renaer explained that Floon had been mistaken for him, dragged unconscious through a cellar trapdoor into the sewer below, and taken to a Xanathar's Guild hideout following yellow chalk guildsigns. Three distinct leads confirmed the route.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/design-notes.md:5: **Seven Events for an Episodic Quest.** Finding Floon has four events because Finding Floon is a linear investigation: it flows from clue to clue, from the surface to the sewers, and ends when Floon is found. Trollskull Alley is structurally different: the party has a home base, multiple concurrent obligations, and no investigation to follow. The seven-event structure imposes narrative progression on open-ended content without prescribing player choices. **Welcome to Trollskull Alley**, **The Guild Gauntlet**, and **The Neighbors** run sequentially because the party cannot receive faction invitations before they have somewhere to receive them. **The Factions Come Calling** overlaps the renovation period with recruitment running parallel to neighbor introductions. **The Field of Triumph** slots into the renovation period: the party is recruited, fighting in the city's arena, and hiring staff all in the same stretch of time. **The Grand Opening** is the renovation's endpoint and the quest's emotional culmination. **The Twin Parades** is the city's calendar, not the party's. It falls when it falls.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/design-notes.md:23: **The Quest's Only Persistent Conflict Thread.** Every other conflict in Trollskull Alley is either friendly (factions) or reactive (guild inspections, neighbor eccentricities). Emmek Frewn is the quest's only genuine antagonist — a man actively working to destroy what the party is building. His escalation sequence runs from guild interference through the Shard Shunner hiring threat and into Stage 4's misfired sabotage at the Twin Parades. He starts in **The Guild Gauntlet** and remains active through the end of the quest.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-01-welcome-to-trollskull-alley.md:97: > The party has established what they own and what it will cost them. Broxley Fairkettle contacts them within the day to deliver the permit checklist. Proceed to **The Guild Gauntlet** Event.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-02-the-guild-gauntlet.md:1: ﻿# The Guild Gauntlet
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-02-the-guild-gauntlet.md:13: > - The Carpenters' Guild negotiation is the quest's first persuasion challenge with a concrete lever
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-02-the-guild-gauntlet.md:19: **Broxley Fairkettle** (Fellowship of Innkeepers) arrives within a day of the party's arrival, permit checklist in hand and apologetic expression already deployed. **Hammond Kraddoc** (Vintners', Distillers', and Brewers' Guild) appears uninvited alongside him, with his scribe Jinny and a second list. Between them, the party has the full picture:
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-02-the-guild-gauntlet.md:21: Required guilds: Carpenters', Roofers', and Plaisterers' Guild (structural work); Cellarers' and Plumbers' Guild (basement and plumbing); Guild of Butchers (meat supply); Bakers' Guild (bread and pastries); Launderers' Guild (linens); Watchful Order of Magists and Protectors (fire suppression ward).
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-02-the-guild-gauntlet.md:27: Frewn spent the day after the party arrived speaking to the Carpenters' Guild foreman and the Cellarers' inspector about the party's "property destruction history" and "possible Watch complications" from the warehouse incident in Finding Floon. These conversations do not block any permit but impose a **−2 penalty on all guild inspection DCs for the first week**. The party will not know the source unless they ask and succeed on a **DC 13 Charisma (Persuasion)** check with the relevant inspector.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-02-the-guild-gauntlet.md:33: ### The Carpenters' Guild Negotiation
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-02-the-guild-gauntlet.md:35: The foreman's quoted renovation timeline is **18 days**. A **DC 14 Charisma (Persuasion)** check or 20 gp above standard fee reduces it to **12 days**. A character with the Guild Artisan background or proficiency in Artisan's Tools reduces the DC by 2.

This is a bluff — he has no capacity to follow through. **DC 13 Wisdom (Insight)** to read it.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-02-the-guild-gauntlet.md:63: **Hammond Kraddoc** appears at the Vintners' Guild inspection, chastises the party's current beverage selection, and suggests two specific guild products "the guild is emphasizing this tenday." Buying both reduces the first month's supply rate by 5 gp. He will never acknowledge this as a social visit.

If the party won the final bout, Xanathar itself has been notified of them — this notification is read by Xanathar's Lair's intelligence briefing and shapes how the lair's occupants respond when the party arrives.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-05-the-field-of-triumph.md:93: The party returned or discarded the token. No further Guild contact in this quest.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-05-the-field-of-triumph.md:97: The party brought the token and the scout's approach to a faction. The faction gains intelligence on Guild scouting patterns in the Sea Ward. Read by Faction Outposts's intelligence-gathering phase.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-05-the-field-of-triumph.md:111: Zardoz Zord invited the party to fight in his sponsored gladiatorial slots at the Field of Triumph. Four rounds of nonlethal combat. The final round put them across the sand from Meloon Wardragon, who shook their hands afterward and asked their names. Xanathar's Guild was watching.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-05-the-field-of-triumph.md:119: The party competed in Zardoz Zord's sponsored gladiatorial slots at the Field of Triumph, met Meloon Wardragon in the final round, and received an unsolicited contact from Xanathar's Guild. The prize purses helped close the renovation gap. The tournament is done; the Grand Opening is next.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/flowchart.md:8: [ev-02] The Guild Gauntlet          ←── Emmek Stage 1 begins (−2 DC penalty)
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/flowchart.md:31: **Welcome to Trollskull Alley, The Guild Gauntlet, and The Neighbors are sequential.** The party cannot receive faction invitations before they have somewhere to receive them. The neighbors cannot introduce themselves before the party arrives. The guild inspectors cannot come before the deed is presented.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/overview.md:29: - **Hammond Kraddoc** (Vintners', Distillers', and Brewers' Guild) — arrives uninvited; his scribe Jinny takes notes on everything
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/overview.md:30: - **Justyn Rassk** (Guild of Butchers) — meat supply inspector; bluff artist; DC 13 Insight to read him correctly
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/overview.md:62: While the renovation ran, Waterdeep came to them. Guild inspectors arrived with clipboards. Neighbors appeared with gifts, concerns, and agendas. Seven adventuring factions sent their own recruiters, targeting specific party members by alignment, race, and reputation — not the group as a whole. Between the guild fees, the renovation costs, and the neighbor across the alley filing complaints about rats, the city showed them exactly what it cost to put down roots.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:55: ***Non-Essential, Consequential.*** ev-06 and ev-07 cannot block the party's path to Gralhund Villa. But a party that pursues them gains: the death mark (characterization of Xanathar's Guild's sophistication), the Cassalanter-to-vault link through Kalain's account of Victoro's visit, the vault-opening method, and the unsigned Eyes letter confirming Neverember's deliberate separation of Stone and Eyes. These are among the most consequential intelligence finds in the campaign, gated behind exactly the investigative habits this quest tries to build: exhaustive canvassing, leaving contact information, following up on letters addressed to dead men.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:61: ***The First Quest Where All Four Villain Factions Pursue the Same Object.*** Finding Floon establishes the Guild and the Zhentarim as adversaries. Trollskull Alley introduces the Cassalanters as patrons and BD as observers. Fireball is the first quest where all four reach for the same object at the same time in the same city. The design makes this concrete: Xanathar gazers in the alley, Zhent parallel investigation, BD conditional observers, Cassalanter condolence note and dinner invitation. These are not flavor — they are the Grand Game becoming visible for the first time.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-01-the-fireball.md:212: - **Xanathar's Guild:** Gazers post to watch Trollskull Alley. DC 16 Wisdom (Perception) to notice a floating eyeball behind a chimney. They are collecting intelligence, not acting.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-06-the-death-mark.md:47: The team consists of two Guild members in civilian clothes. They probe what the party knows, not what the party found. They share intelligence in exchange for intelligence, unconsciously — each piece the party provides yields a fragment in return.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-06-the-death-mark.md:62: > A party that already knows the Gralhund address gains nothing from this track beyond characterization of the Guild's operational sophistication.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-06-the-death-mark.md:68: **Mirt (Harpers, Renown 1+):** Identifies the mark without hesitation — he has seen them before. Shares a rough map of observed Guild patrol patterns in the Trades Ward. Notes that Harper intelligence in this district may be tainted, because the other half of the Black Network seems to hear what the Harpers learn. He says this in a low voice and does not elaborate.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-06-the-death-mark.md:70: **Vajra Safahr (Force Grey, Renown 1+):** Interested in what the mark implies about the Guild's operational sophistication — they were tracking Dalakhar in his lodgings. She has a partial dossier on Dalakhar: confirmed his role as Neverember's agent, notes he infiltrated Xanathar's organization before going dark. **DC 13 Charisma (Persuasion)** to receive the dossier. It confirms Dalakhar was already a known quantity to Force Grey; they were not acting on the information.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-06-the-death-mark.md:72: **Jalester Silvermane (Lords' Alliance, Renown 1+):** Reports to Laeral Silverhand. Returns within two hours with a confirmation: Guild is operating above street level, possible Watch leak involved in the soft inquiry at Gralhund Villa. Offers 72-hour Watch ID papers (advantage on Persuasion checks with constables). Notes independently — as a third path to the Gralhund address if needed — that a North Ward household matching Gralhund Villa's description has been flagged as a possible Black Network safe house.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-06-the-death-mark.md:74: **Davil Starsong (Doom Raiders, Renown 1+):** Examines the mark with professional detachment. Confirms Guild death marks have been increasing over the last month in the Trades Ward. He is calculating whether the same intelligence leak that exposed Dalakhar's movements might expose his own cell's operations next — and whether Floxin filed the anonymous Watch tip naming the Doom Raiders as persons of interest, drawing Watch heat away from the Splinter cell. He does not share this calculation with the party unless pressed (DC 13 Charisma (Persuasion)).
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-06-the-death-mark.md:107: Dalakhar's last room at the Inn of the Dripping Dagger held ash from burned correspondence, marks of a surveillance team's entry, and Xanathar's death mark on the pillow — left for a man who never came back. The Guild was watching him. So was everyone else.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-07-kalainsalley-tower.md:59: **2. Kraddoc correspondence.** Letters to Hammond Kraddoc of the Vintners', Distillers', and Brewers' Guild confirming large bribes paid to cover a contaminated-liquor scandal in the Dock Ward. Neverember's private leverage over the Guild. Not directly relevant to the Stone but potentially useful for a party building political assets.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-08-the-rooftop-chase.md:61: **Response team intercept:** If the GM wants a harder version of this scene, a Xanathar's Guild response team cuts off the rooftop exit route, producing a three-way confrontation for the Stone. Refer to the Response Teams section of the **Xanathar's Guild** Factions guide page for roster and tactics.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-09-aftermath.md:111: **Lords' Alliance (Jalester).** His debrief request arrives within twenty-four hours. The Watch officers on the Saerdoun Street beat were transferred to a Far South Ward assignment the week before the raid. Jalester has traced the reassignment to a request from inside the Watch hierarchy; the source is unknown. He wants to know if the party saw any evidence of Watch coordination with Xanathar's Guild or the Gralhund household.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-09-aftermath.md:113: **Emerald Enclave (Melannor).** Gralhund Villa sits over a minor ley line the Enclave monitors as part of Jeryth Phaulkon's aberrant-activity survey. Xanathar's Guild's presence in the neighborhood destabilized it over the past week in a way that correlates with Nihiloor's movements below the city. Melannor wants to know what the party observed. For the map.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-09-aftermath.md:117: **Force Grey (Vajra).** A full operational debrief: which factions were present, where each one entered and exited, whether any Watch checkpoint in the surrounding blocks was compromised or unmanned during the raid, and any direct evidence of Nihiloor's involvement. Xanathar's Guild activity approaching a Watch checkpoint the Guild has never previously targeted is, in her judgment, a test. She wants to know if the test succeeded.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/design-notes.md:5: The exposé comes from the source: a lurid piece on devil worship and orgies among unnamed Waterdhavian noble families, written by Jarlaxle and delivered by break-in. The restored draft made it accurate. It described a ritual space matching the Cassalanter villa's lower temple and credited a witness account to Nar'l Xibrindas, so Xanathar's Guild would have known the Cassalanters were infernalists before the party did. That contradicts the standing rule that no other faction knows.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/design-notes.md:29: Gaxly appears in two faction chains: this one and **The Black Viper Investigation** for the Order of the Gauntlet. The older draft also listed Doom Raiders and Xanathar's Guild. The Doom Raiders notes retire him, and no Xanathar mission uses him. A party that has run both missions meets a man who has had two people leave things on his desk and a third ask him about it. He isn't corrupt. He prints what sells and what he can half verify, which is why every faction can use him.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/design-notes.md:7: Leaving the cause open means the mission needs another reason for the party to care, and the captain's note supplies it. Jarlaxle did not set the trap, but he saw its use. Xanathar cannot do without the one man who feeds Sylgar, so the three nights show what the Guild will spend to get him back, and Fel'rekt counts it from the roof of the Bent Nail. The party never learns Fel'rekt is there unless it looks, and he never comes down. Krebbyg is a poor source and says so, which keeps the captain's real interest out of reach until later events.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/design-notes.md:11: The source sends six bugbears. Six 2024 Bugbear Warriors at once are a Devastating fight for four 4th-level characters and an Impossible one for three, so the audit scales the roster to the number of characters who fight. Three combatants face a Bugbear Warrior and a Tough, four face two Bugbear Warriors and a Bandit, and five face three Bugbear Warriors. The rest of the gang waits at the alley mouth with the cart and runs when the first group falls, which keeps both the Guild's six and the party's survival true.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/design-notes.md:19: The plain Dungsweepers keep the table at four as the source describes, and the roster uses them to scale the fight. Keeping every plain worker alive earns a Renown bonus, which gives the party a reason to look at the table before it acts. Three independent clues reach the same conclusion. They are Ott's own account of the Guild's tools, a DC 15 Wisdom (Insight) or Wisdom (Perception) check at the table, and the fixed 23:00 move for a party that misses both. The devourers never use Steal Body on a fallen character, because the stat block would otherwise let one failed save end a character's story. The text says so plainly.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/design-notes.md:38: Ott vanishes with the bands and reappears in Xanathar's Lair by morning with no explanation, because the Faction Outposts draft has him strapped to a chair in the Interrogation House after being captured in the Guild war. That entry calls him a gnome, which the source and his Notable Figures page contradict, but it needs him to be a Guild man the Zhentarim could take, and this mission gives it one. **Ott Kept** and **Ott Lost** both lead to the same chair, and the only difference is whether the Guild knows Trollskull Manor held him.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/design-notes.md:47: - Invented details are Fel'rekt on the roof, the Guild's cart at the alley mouth, the flying snake's blue-and-orange scales, the 30-foot distance from the grate to Ott's corner, the six-round clock and Lif's four-effect list.

Ott knows the Guild's ways and tells the party what is coming when asked.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/ev-01-three-nights.md:171: The Guild moves first with its muscle. Six bugbears set out from the docks before midnight with a hired mule and cart. They reach the alley as the shrine bell strikes twelve. The courtyard gate opens onto the kitchen, and the cellar door is in the kitchen corridor beyond it, so the courtyard group is the one that threatens Ott. The front door group only has to hold the taproom.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/ev-01-three-nights.md:238: > He answers in short sentences and fears the Master more than he fears the party. He knows only what the Guild told him and nothing about the devourers or the zombie.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/ev-01-three-nights.md:287: > Some of the Dungsweepers' Guild workers at the back table were taken in the old cisterns about a tenday ago by devourers from Nihiloor's stock. A 2024 **Intellect Devourer** that takes a body consumes the host's brain, so each host died when it was taken, and nothing the party does can save it. The devourers control the dead bodies, which still know the workers' names, languages and routes.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/ev-01-three-nights.md:289: > Any character who examines a fallen host and makes a successful DC 13 Wisdom (Medicine) check finds that the host's skull holds no brain and has held none for days. The Dungsweepers who carry no devourer are ordinary workers. They were told to come to a Guild night out and keep to their seats, and they know nothing.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/ev-01-three-nights.md:322: > A Dungsweepers' Guild Commoner (Neutral, Waterdhavian Human, she/her) :: a sewer sweeper in her fifties who does not like her crew's quiet guests and is relieved to be asked.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/ev-01-three-nights.md:328: > > "They came to our crew from the old cisterns about a tenday ago. None of them has eaten with us since, or taken off their boots, or said a word. The foreman told us it was a Guild night out and to keep our mouths shut. I thought it was a bad idea, but you don't argue with the foreman."
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/ev-01-three-nights.md:350: > The Guild sent a 2024 **Beholder Zombie** up the old sewer line to the grate in the cellar floor, the nearest way to the cellar. Part of the old tunnel fell on it on the way. The **Bregan D'aerthe Mechanics Reference** audits the fight and flags it as a High-Power encounter. Four changes bring it within reach of 4th-level characters.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/ev-01-three-nights.md:391: If the zombie is still alive, it hangs over the hole for a moment with its eyes rolling, and then it turns and sinks out of sight the way it came. The hole in the cellar floor stays, and the Cellarers' and Plumbers' Guild inspector who flagged the old grate will want to see it.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/ev-01-three-nights.md:397: The Event concludes when the bands take Ott, or when the Guild gets him first. The second black pouch arrives that afternoon in **Coin Pouches**, and Krebbyg does not come for Ott.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/ev-01-three-nights.md:399: If **Ott Lost** is marked before the third night, the remaining nights do not happen. Ott returns to his bowl in the lair and tells the Guild that Trollskull Manor held him. If the bands take Ott, he reappears in Xanathar's fish room by morning with no memory of how he got there. He tells the Guild that humans held him in a cellar and that he does not know where. The Zhentarim take him during the Guild war.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/ev-01-three-nights.md:413: > - **Ott Kept** — mark when the bands take Ott away while he is still in the party's keeping, whether he is in the cellar or elsewhere; read by **Faction Outposts** (unconverted), where Ott is a prisoner of the Zhentarim at the Interrogation House after being taken in the Guild war.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/ev-01-three-nights.md:414: > - **Ott Lost** — mark when the Guild's bugbears, hosts or zombie carry Ott out of the party's keeping before the bands take him; read by **Faction Outposts** (unconverted), where the Guild already knows Trollskull Manor held him.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/overview.md:23: Jarlaxle Baenre learned of the guest at dawn from his watchers on the alley and wrote the note himself. Xanathar cannot do without the one man who knows how to keep Sylgar alive, and Jarlaxle wants to see what the Guild spends to get him back. Fel'rekt Lafeen watches from the roof of the Bent Nail each night and counts what comes through the doors. He does not interfere.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/overview.md:31: At midnight the Guild's bugbears come for Ott through the front door and the courtyard gate. Six bugbears at once would flatten a party of this level, so only part of the gang comes in. The rest wait at the alley mouth with a cart and run when the first ones fall. The party can fight at the doors, pay the gang to leave, or empty the cellar before they arrive.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/overview.md:35: Four Dungsweepers' Guild workers sit down at the back table in the evening and say nothing. Some of them carry intellect devourers, which sense minds through walls and know exactly where Ott is. The party can spot them, move Ott out of their reach, clear the taproom or wait for them to make their move at 23:00.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/overview.md:43: Each participating Bregan D'aerthe member gains 3 base Renown if Ott is still in the party's keeping when the bands take him, and 1 base Renown if the Guild gets him first. Bonuses of +1 each go to members who keep the bugbears out of the cellar on Night One, who lose no uninfected Dungsweeper on Night Two, and who stop the zombie from lifting Ott on Night Three.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m03-three-nights/overview.md:47: Ott vanishes with the bands, and Krebbyg does not collect him. The second black pouch arrives that afternoon in **Coin Pouches**. Ott turns up again in Xanathar's Lair and is later taken by the Zhentarim during the Guild war, which is how he comes to be in the Interrogation House when **Faction Outposts** begins. **The Compromised Eye** becomes available at Renown 8 and 5th level.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m04-the-compromised-eye/design-notes.md:27: Tarn Hobble and Dorrim Ketch are invented, as is Arannis Nur'zekk's role as spokesman for the four drow. WDH names the four drow and does not say which of them speaks. Nevercott has no voice profile, and his letter is written from his persona in the First Meeting. The grell, the Guild's response and Ahmaergo's clerks have no profiles and speak only where needed.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m04-the-compromised-eye/ev-01-the-compromised-eye.md:20: > - Xanathar told Ahmaergo to find out what is wrong with its advisor. Ahmaergo's clerks have built a case from three Dock Ward incident reports that Nar'l never forwarded, and from a fence named Tarn Hobble, who told them a drow in a Guild coat was seen near the quay on two of the nights the elves died.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m04-the-compromised-eye/ev-01-the-compromised-eye.md:30: > - **Soluun Expelled** (marked by **The Killer's Fate**): The captain has put Soluun ashore, and the disownment that began as a cover story is now true. Nobody in this Event calls it a cover story. Nar'l is relieved and ashamed of the relief, and he knows the Guild's case exists because of what his brother did.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m04-the-compromised-eye/ev-01-the-compromised-eye.md:34: > The killings affect the discredit route in **Twelve Minutes**. If Soluun was killed, the Guild's watchers know the killings have stopped, and the replacement reports are easier to believe. If neither is marked, the killer is still loose in the Dock Ward, and they are harder.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m04-the-compromised-eye/ev-01-the-compromised-eye.md:38: > - **Soluun Pressed (Brother):** The member heard Soluun say that his brother has spent two months burying his trail with the Guild's own paper, so the member arrives suspecting what the reports hold. Every Insight DC in **Is He Telling the Truth?** is 4 lower for that member.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m04-the-compromised-eye/overview.md:10: > Nar'l Xibrindas uses the **Mage** stat block and wants to talk, and his grell bodyguard uses the **Grell**. campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m04-the-compromised-eye/overview.md:21: Nar'l has served as Xanathar's drow advisor for a year and has reported to Bregan D'aerthe by *Sending* every third day. Xanathar became suspicious and gave him a grell bodyguard with orders to kill him at the first sign of disloyalty, and the grell keeps Nar'l from sending or receiving anything unobserved. His elder brother Soluun is the reason the Guild has a case against him. The Dock Ward killings were Soluun's work, Nar'l buried the reports that would have pointed at a drow, and Ahmaergo's clerks noticed the gap.

If the case is discredited, Xanathar loses its only reason to act, and the grell's order goes on waiting for a sign that never comes.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m06-the-dive/design-notes.md:5: ***The mission used to recover an Eye from a wreck, and now it defends a boat.*** The restored draft sank a ketch with Eye #3 aboard and sent the party after it, racing a Guild dive team. That contradicted campaign canon in two ways. The lair heists and **Sea Maidens Faire** decide who holds the Eyes, and **Xanathar's Lair** has the beholder not knowing what the Eyes are. It also sent a 7th-level party, after all four heists, to recover an Eye they had already collected or lost. The mission now follows the Factions Guide. Xanathar's divers clamp a limpet charge to the *Scarlet Marpenoth*, and the party removes it before dawn. The event never says whether the submarine carries an Eye, so it stays order-agnostic about who holds them.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m06-the-dive/design-notes.md:7: ***The Guild is after an asset, not a prize.*** Ahmaergo treats Bregan D'aerthe as the Guild's enemy and a submarine as the one thing the captain cannot replace. Nothing in this mission can be picked up and carried off, so the stakes stay on the boat and the people around it.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m06-the-dive/design-notes.md:11: ***The Faire left on Tarsakh 20.*** The draft had the *Eyecatcher* anchored 200 yards from a pier, and Krebbyg saying the Faire was committed to Waterdeep for another season. Neither fits the calendar, which closes the Faire's paths on Tarsakh 20 and has the ships leave on schedule. The event now has the first mate take the *Eyecatcher* while the captain stays ashore with Krebbyg, Fel'rekt and a small team. The arc-h structure doc puts a drow mage, not the captain, at the *Eyecatcher*'s helm, so Zardoz Zord can stay in Waterdeep without contradiction. The submarine goes to a reserve berth under the outer end of the old Faire pier at Smugglers' Dock, 20 feet down. Soluun knew that berth because he slept aboard, so a sale made before the Faire sailed fits the mooring the Guild found.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m06-the-dive/design-notes.md:15: ***Three sources, one question.*** The Guild finds the berth through one of three sources, and the party can learn each one:
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m06-the-dive/design-notes.md:18: - **Nar'l's grief**, if **Soluun Killed** is marked with **Nar'l Active** or **Nar'l Cleared**. Orlo says a soft-spoken drow in a Guild coat gave the Guild the berth.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m06-the-dive/design-notes.md:48: ***Orlo and Nell are plain people.*** Orlo is a salvage diver with no love for the Guild's clerks, and Nell is a professional lookout with a price. Neither is a villain, and both can be bought, talked down or fooled, which lets a faction mission about a bomb on a boat avoid a slaughter.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m06-the-dive/design-notes.md:54: ***Soluun Sold the Mooring and Guild Survivor Escaped.*** The first gives **Houseless Noble** the empty seat's account, in a paired readaloud for **Soluun Expelled** with **Soluun Sold the Mooring**, and triggers the plea penalty. The second tells **Vault of Dragons** Scene 5 that the Guild knows which members dived.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m06-the-dive/ev-01-the-dive.md:186: > Nell has watched harbors for the Guild for six years. She is calm and professional, she doesn't want to die for the dive leader's bonus, and she flashes the lantern the moment she sees anyone who isn't the dive team. She can be bought if the price is right and the threat is credible, because she knows how Ahmaergo's clerks treat anyone who brings back a failure. campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/r50-houseless-noble/design-notes.md:47: The draft's favor was "I genuinely do not know yet," which left the GM to improvise. The Event now settles it. It offers a safe road for an Auvryndar courier in exchange for Xanathar's Guild mooring list. The cost is the secret, which the member keeps from any Zhentarim or Doom Raider ally in the party until Rizzeryl has read it. This is the old draft's "keeping a secret that conflicts with a party ally's interests" category, and it asks nothing of a companion. The host does not describe the favor because telling would change how the member carries it.

campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/r03-wolf/ev-01-wolf.md:24: > The informant web is real. It is made up of dockhands, fences, two clerks at the Halls of Justice, and Tashlyn's reports from her post at the South Gate, and its information on Xanathar's Guild is good. Its information on the other cell is good about the cell's teams and poor about the cell's master. The Doom Raiders believe Floxin leads it, when Floxin is Manshoon's field agent and takes his orders from him. Nobody in this Event can say Manshoon's name unless **Manshoon Named** has been marked, and the web reports every cell fact as "the other cell" or "the Splinter", and adds "Floxin's cell" only if **Floxin Status** is Alive. If **Floxin Status** is Dead or Captured, Davil or Tashlyn tells the member that the man they took for the leader is gone and the cell still moves, so someone above him is giving the orders and they do not know who.

Three DC 12 Intimidation successes before three failures disperse the thugs, a DC 12 Dexterity check lets the party signal Pell, and Pell later owes a favor and knows which Field Ward gang leaders the Splinter has paid. The source is a single use-of-force problem with a reward and no debrief. The restored draft kept all of it, added one report line and left Pell's favor without a reader.

campaign/setting/deities/milil.md:78: > - **Lysa of the White Lute:** A neutral good half-elf and the second House Bard candidate in the Trollskull Manor staff table. campaign/setting/history/00-the-history.md:20: > - Three of the four villain factions came to Waterdeep from elsewhere. Xanathar's Guild rose from Skullport, Bregan D'aerthe came by way of Menzoberranzan and Luskan, and the Zhentarim came from Zhentil Keep.
campaign/setting/history/00-the-history.md:29: - **The Long Shadow** (by 1357 DR to 1492 DR): Xanathar as a title held by a line of beholders, the current one named Zushaxx, and Skullport as the Guild's base. It sets up **Xanathar's Lair** and the crypt of past holders.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:8: > - **What this is:** The Era page for the history of Xanathar's Guild and of Skullport, the undercity it rules from. It is the second of eight eras, after **Ahghairon's Gifts** and before **Bregan D'aerthe's Long Road**.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:9: > - **Where it is used:** The GM reads it before voicing Xanathar, Ahmaergo or Nar'l Xibrindas, before answering a contact's question about where the Guild comes from, and before running area X33 of **Xanathar's Lair**, the crypt of the previous Xanathars.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:13: **Purpose:** Lets the GM answer any question about why Xanathar is a title, how the Guild remembers or forgets its past, what Skullport is to it and what the lair's crypt proves.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:15: **Keywords:** Xanathar, Xanathar's Guild, Zushaxx, Kirukesai, Skullport, Sargauth Enclave, Netherese, Skulls of Skullport, Ahmaergo, Nar'l Xibrindas, Nihiloor, Crypt of Xanathars Past
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:17: **Span:** By 1357 DR to 1492 DR, about 135 years of Guild history. The Forgotten Realms Wiki first shows the Guild thriving as a thieves' guild in 1357 DR, and no source gives its founding year. `[research]` The old era block said "a century". Skullport itself reaches back to about -750 DR. The campaign opens on Ches 1, 1492 DR.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:19: **Other Names:** Xanathar's Guild is the campaign name. The published adventure drops the possessive and the wiki calls it Xanathar's Thieves' Guild. The wiki also gives the Agents of the Eye as the older Skullport name, used in 1372 DR. No in-world name for the era itself is sourced. `[research]`
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:24: > - "Xanathar" is a title held by a succession of beholders. Each holder inherits the Guild's apparatus and its paranoia together. The current Xanathar is a beholder named Zushaxx, and its pronouns are "it". Kirukesai was one of the previous holders.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:25: > - The Guild keeps continuity by eliminating anyone who knows more than the current Xanathar. The campaign line is that Zushaxx believes it is the original. The crypt in the lair shows the title is a succession, and the user has ruled that the remix line wins over the published room.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:26: > - Skullport is the Guild's undercity base. Netherese wizards built it more than two thousand years before the campaign, many masters have held it since, and the Guild holds it in 1492 DR.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:28: > - At campaign start the Guild is an empire built on fear, run by a paranoid intelligence and served by people who survive by hiding their ambitions behind obedience.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:35: - The wiki's account of the first holder: a beholder hired by the Zhentarim to start a cell in Skullport. It cut them off, built its own guild, and by 1357 DR the Guild was a thieves' guild working through freelance agents under four Councilors. Each Councilor supervised one domain: mercenaries, bookkeeping, thievery or blackmail (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Xanathar's Thieves' Guild; Waterdeep and the North). `[research: wiki only]`
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:38: - The only account of how Zushaxx took the title is the wiki's: Izulktur replaced itself with Zushaxx, with no method or date given (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Xanathar's Thieves' Guild and Xanathar; Dungeon #206). No campaign page adds to it.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:40: ### Guild Structure and Continuity
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:42: - The Guild keeps continuity by systematically eliminating anyone who knows more than the current Xanathar. What remains is a surveillance network with no institutional memory beyond what the current beholder chooses to believe.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:45: - The ranks, lowest to highest, are Eyestalker, Agent of the Eye, Eye Ray, Guild Boss and Hand of the Eye (WDH).
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:47: - The Dock Ward is the Guild's primary surface territory.

campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:60: - The sourced chain of masters runs from a Netherese enclave to the Guild. Netherese wizards of the Sargauth Enclave built the place about -750 DR. Halaster Blackcloak's apprentice Shradin Mulophor settled it in 1148 DR, and the Keepers took control of its sea locks in 1166 to 1168 DR. The Skulls destroyed Shradin in 1369 DR, and the Spellplague of 1385 DR gutted the old magical mantle. Mithral revived the town late in the 15th century. `[research: wiki only]`
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:61: - The published adventure lists the town's earlier occupants as duergar, drow, dwarves, crazed wizards, slavers and pirates. Today it is under the sway of Xanathar, and the businesses that remain pay the Guild for protection.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:62: - Thirteen flameskulls, the remnants of the Sargauth Enclave, still haunt the town and consider themselves its true rulers. They rarely communicate, and by 1492 DR they do not interfere with the Guild.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:66: - The Guild keeps watch posts on the upper levels of Undermountain, staffed by bugbears and goblins, to stop adventurers reaching the lower levels (WDMM).
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:67: - The Guild has rivals below. A drow mage named Rizzeryl works for House Auvryndar and for the Zhentarim, and he and his wererats harry Guild forces on one of the upper levels (WDMM). Which Zhentarim he serves is not settled for the remix, and Draft Notes lists it.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:69: ### The Guild at Campaign Start
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:71: - The Guild is an empire built on fear, run by a paranoid intelligence and served by people who survive by hiding their ambitions behind obedience.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:78: Xanathar's Guild is the largest criminal organization in Waterdeep and, according to rumor, the only one led by a beholder. Its roots lie in Skullport, an outlaw port in the caverns below the city, reached through Undermountain and outside the Watch's jurisdiction, which Netherese wizards built long ago and which many masters have held since. Its informants work in every ward, and its main territory above ground is the Dock Ward. It is said that many of its own members have never seen their master.

campaign/setting/history/08-the-players-arrive.md:75: Waterdeep enters the spring of 1492 DR as a prosperous city with a restless underworld. Brawls between the Xanathar's Guild and the Zhentarim have become common in the Dock Ward, and the City Watch is called to them often. Several noble households have taken delivery of mechanical servants bought from a foreign dealer, and the city is preparing for Fleetswake, the maritime festival that fills the harbor at the end of Ches.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/01-davil-starsong.md:16: **Motivations.** Davil wants to keep the Doom Raiders operational and independent from Manshoon's splinter, maintain peace with Xanathar's Guild through negotiation rather than violence, and protect the people who chose to follow him.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/harpers/05-corene-wyldath.md:26: **Relationships.** Corene is a Harper deep-cover agent embedded in the Dock Ward operation of Xanathar's Guild, the subject of Harper mission five (**The Sleeping Asset**), and the most dangerous kind of asset: one who cannot confirm her own loyalty.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/05-meloon-wardragon.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Force Grey (occupied by a Xanathar's Guild intellect devourer after the Field of Triumph, about three tendays before the first day of the watch)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/05-meloon-wardragon.md:26: **Relationships.** Meloon is an active member of Force Grey (reporting to Vajra Safahr, whose life he saved more than a decade ago alongside Renaer), the closest friend of Renaer Neverember (whose growing unease, without knowing why, becomes alarm once the occupation takes hold), a Yawning Portal regular whose surface thoughts Bonnie reads as comprehensibly normal until the occupation, and, after the Field of Triumph, a host for Nihiloor's intellect devourer, which reports to the Guild through a courier because telepathy reaches only 60 feet.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/06-dalakhar.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Dalakhar was a small gnome with dry waste on his boots and sewer-stink on his cloak. To those he trusted, he was Neverember's most reliable agent, the one who succeeded where many others had failed. Deep down, he was afraid that the Zhentarim and Xanathar's Guild were close to catching him, and he was right.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/trollskull-community/04-fala-lefaliir.md:26: **Relationships.** Fala is a close friend of Ziraj the Hunter (Doom Raiders assassin, who keeps a room on Fala's second floor), a neighbor and confidant of Tally Fellbranch, and a member of the Guild of Apothecaries and Physicians. Fala uses they/them pronouns, reflecting Corellon Larethian's genderless nature.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/trollskull-community/07-hammond-kraddoc.md:16: **Motivations.** Kraddoc wants to maintain the Vintners', Distillers', and Brewers' Guild's grip on the North Ward spirit trade, prove that he knows more about drink than anyone the party employs, and visit socially without admitting that is what he is doing.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/trollskull-community/07-hammond-kraddoc.md:26: **Relationships.** Kraddoc is accompanied everywhere by his scribe Jinny (a young tiefling who records everything), a recurring antagonist of Emmek Frewn (whose establishment he also inspects, with less patience), and a representative of the Vintners', Distillers', and Brewers' Guild.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/trollskull-community/07-hammond-kraddoc.md:30: Kraddoc visits the tavern as the Vintners', Distillers', and Brewers' Guild representative, arriving with clipboard, scribe, and opinions about the party's spirits selection — and staying, when no inspection is needed, simply because he has nowhere better to be.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/01-xanathar.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Xanathar's Guild
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/01-xanathar.md:27: **Relationships.** Xanathar is the beholder crime lord of Xanathar's Guild, the employer of Ahmaergo (its majordomo), Nihiloor (its mind flayer, which has psionically dominated Xanathar for the last four months without Xanathar knowing), and Nar'l Xibrindas (its drow advisor, whom it does not yet know is Jarlaxle's spy). It has a healthy fear of Laeral Silverhand and spares those in her employ.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/02-ahmaergo.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Xanathar's Guild
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/02-ahmaergo.md:16: **Motivations.** Ahmaergo wants to maintain his position as the most influential member of the Guild after Xanathar, expand his minotaur collection, and run the Guild's operations with the efficiency that keeps the beholder's paranoia pointed at others.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/02-ahmaergo.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Ahmaergo is outwardly civil, a shield dwarf who handles logistics, procurement, and discipline with administrative calm. To those in the Guild, he is as devious and corrupt as the worst devil, yet unflinchingly loyal to Xanathar. Deep down, his quarters feature a maze of stacked crates, a stuffed minotaur, skeletal remains arranged as decor, and a bed made from minotaur skulls, bones, hide, and fur. The fascination is total.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/02-ahmaergo.md:24: **Morale.** In a fight, Ahmaergo commands Guild forces and fights with vicious competence. He does not retreat while Xanathar is present, and he responds to the beholder's screamed orders without hesitation.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/02-ahmaergo.md:26: **Relationships.** Ahmaergo is Xanathar's majordomo (most orders that bear Xanathar's name were written by Ahmaergo), the Guild's de facto second-in-command, and a shield dwarf whose minotaur obsession fills every corner of his quarters.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/02-ahmaergo.md:30: Ahmaergo is the outward face of Xanathar's Guild for administrative matters — a civil shield dwarf who manages procurement, discipline, and operations with methodical competence. He is as polite as he is dangerous.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/03-narl-xibrindas.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Xanathar's Guild (secretly Bregan D'aerthe)
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/03-narl-xibrindas.md:16: **Motivations.** Nar'l wants to survive his assignment as Jarlaxle's intelligence asset inside Xanathar's Guild, avoid Xanathar's suspicion (which is growing), and keep his options open in case he needs to betray Bregan D'Aerthe to save himself.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/03-narl-xibrindas.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Nar'l is Xanathar's trusted drow advisor who convinced the beholder to eliminate its other counselors. To Jarlaxle, he is Bregan D'Aerthe's best intelligence source on Guild movements, reporting via Sending spell after one year in place, placed in 1491 DR. Deep down, Xanathar suspects a mole and has assigned Nar'l a grell bodyguard with orders to kill him at the first sign of disloyalty. If forced to choose between himself and Bregan D'Aerthe, he will choose himself.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/03-narl-xibrindas.md:26: **Relationships.** Nar'l is the younger brother of Soluun Xibrindas (who considers him a weakling), Jarlaxle's intelligence asset inside Xanathar's Guild, and Xanathar's only advisor, who has a grell bodyguard assigned to execute him at the first sign of treachery.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/04-nihiloor.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Xanathar's Guild
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/04-nihiloor.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Nihiloor is Xanathar's mind flayer operative. To Xanathar, it is a trusted mind flayer operative with a status no other Guild member holds. Deep down, Nihiloor has psionically dominated Xanathar for the last four months, and Xanathar does not know. It breeds intellect devourers, sets them loose in the sewers, and uses them to occupy Waterdavians, who walk the city with their own brains intact and a devourer steering them. Its methods leave little behind for PCs to question. Meloon Wardragon and Watch clerk Orvyn Dall are among its current hosts.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/04-nihiloor.md:26: **Relationships.** Nihiloor is the intelligence and infiltration specialist of Xanathar's Guild, the creator of the intellect devourer that occupies Meloon Wardragon, and a mind flayer that has dominated Xanathar's mind for the last four months in a Guild where even a hint of that would get anyone else disintegrated.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/04-nihiloor.md:30: Nihiloor is the intelligence operative of Xanathar's Guild — a mind flayer who moves through the lair with unsettling calm, treating every conversation as an opportunity for study. The Guild's other members give it wide berth.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/05-noska-urgray.md:6: > - **Affiliation:** Xanathar's Guild
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/05-noska-urgray.md:15: **Motivations.** Noska wants to hurt people Xanathar tells him to hurt, maintain his position as the Guild's heaviest response option, and feed his rust monster.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/05-noska-urgray.md:21: **Persona.** To the world, Noska is a ruthless enforcer with a heavy crossbow replacing his left forearm (lost to a green slime in Undermountain). To those in the Guild, he is sent when Xanathar is done sending messages and wants the problem gone. Deep down, he keeps a rust monster as a pet and uses mannequins for target practice. He sleeps in a converted bathtub padded with straw and furs. His inner life is exactly as simple as it appears.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/05-noska-urgray.md:25: **Relationships.** Noska is an enforcer of Xanathar's Guild, the third response team leader, and a shield dwarf whose crossbow-arm, rust monster pet, and bathtub bed constitute a complete portrait of his priorities.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/05-noska-urgray.md:29: Noska Ur'gray is an enforcer of Xanathar's Guild, recognizable by the heavy crossbow that replaces his left forearm. He is direct, uncomplicated, and reliably present whenever the Guild needs a problem solved by violence.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/06-ott-steeltoes.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Xanathar's Guild
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/06-ott-steeltoes.md:26: **Relationships.** Ott is Xanathar's fishkeeper (all Sylgar replacements have passed through him), a Zuggtmoy worshipper who cultivates mushrooms in his quarters, a Zhentarim prisoner at the Interrogation House, a field agent of Xanathar's Guild, and a detailed intelligence source for anyone who rescues him and asks the right questions about the Lair.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/06-ott-steeltoes.md:30: Ott Steeltoes is a Guild functionary best known for wearing a hat fashioned from a beholder's dried eyestalks. He tends the fish — a quiet task with impossible stakes — and otherwise keeps to himself.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/07-thorvin-twinbeard.md:30: Thorvin Twinbeard is an irritable dwarf engineer employed by Xanathar's Guild to maintain its mechanical constructs and devices. He takes no questions about his methods or his employers.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/08-keln-trigos.md:6: > - *Tiefling priest (Xanathar's Guild), neutral evil*. Stat block: **Priest**.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/08-keln-trigos.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Xanathar's Guild
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/08-keln-trigos.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Keln Trigos runs a quiet healing operation in a Castle Ward rowhouse and asks no questions about how you got hurt. To those who know the passphrases, the building runs two entirely separate services that have never been officially linked, and both function without incident. Deep down, he has worked for Xanathar long enough to understand what happens to people the Guild stops using, and he is therefore very reliable.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/08-keln-trigos.md:26: **Relationships.** Keln is the Tier 1 outpost keeper of Xanathar's Guild (Terasse Estate, Elsambul's Lane, Castle Ward), the manager of the gladiatorial transport route (healing services and Lair access under the same roof), and the keeper of two passphrases that access very different services.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/09-grinda-garloth.md:26: **Relationships.** Grinda Garloth is a black market armorer and salvage wizard at Mistshore, an allied service provider to both Xanathar's Guild and Bregan D'Aerthe, the owner of the Apparatus of Kwalish, and an emergency combat support option in the Stone of Golorr investigation if the party defended her home.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/10-korgstrod-uxgulm.md:30: Korgstrod Uxgulm is a duergar who holds court in a stone chair attended by kobold lackeys, plainly expecting obedience and receiving it. He handles Guild business with blunt authority.
