campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/12-grevik-nass.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Xanathar's Guild
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/12-grevik-nass.md:16: **Motivations.** Grevik wants to compile an accurate patron intelligence file for Xanathar's Guild (who dines here, with whom, and about what), make the food worth recommending so no one looks closely at the cook, and complete the assignment without drawing a replacement.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/12-grevik-nass.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Grevik is a half-orc cook who lets the food do the talking: attentive to the pass, quiet at the bar, no trouble to manage. To those who work alongside him, he notes which tables order twice, which guests lower their voices, and which visitors he sees again without a reservation. Deep down, he has filed more detailed intelligence from this kitchen than the Guild expected, and the thought that the tally might make him harder to release than to retire has started to surface at quiet moments.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/12-grevik-nass.md:24: **Morale.** In a fight, Grevik yields position, retreats through the kitchen, and gets a runner to the Guild within the hour. He is an intelligence operative and fights nothing he can avoid.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/12-grevik-nass.md:26: **Relationships.** Grevik is an operative of Xanathar's Guild embedded as the tavern's Master Cook candidate, a man whose two-tenday file will give Xanathar a usable picture of Trollskull's patronage, and one of two Guild plants in the tavern staff alongside Tobrin Ashvale, whose position behind the bar gives the Guild a second angle on the same room.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/13-tobrin-ashvale.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Xanathar's Guild
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/13-tobrin-ashvale.md:16: **Motivations.** Tobrin wants to maintain a running intelligence file on Trollskull's patrons, visitors, and overheard conversations; keep the bar warm enough that no one suspects the bartender; and use his position in the Guild's response chain to trigger escalation when the threshold is crossed.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/13-tobrin-ashvale.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Tobrin is a warm, attentive bartender who makes everyone feel like a regular. To those he trusts (meaning no one), he runs a rolling intelligence file on every patron: who meets whom, who seems nervous, who the party has been talking to. Deep down, he is one of two Guild plants in the building, the one with better social access, and he is waiting to see how long it takes anyone to notice either of them.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/13-tobrin-ashvale.md:24: **Morale.** In a fight, Tobrin does not fight. He disappears into the crowd and reports at the first opportunity. Once discovered, he negotiates: what he knows about the Guild's operations in this building is worth more to the party than his Guild debt, and he understands the math.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/13-tobrin-ashvale.md:26: **Relationships.** Tobrin is an operative of Xanathar's Guild embedded as the tavern's bartender, one of the Guild's two Trollskull infiltrators alongside Grevik Nass, and the trigger for the Guild's escalating response conditions (detailed in the **Trollskull Manor** guide); when the party crosses a threshold, Tobrin makes the call.

campaign/setting/organizations/08-xanathars-guild.md:1: # Xanathar's Guild
campaign/setting/organizations/08-xanathars-guild.md:15: Its most visible eccentricity is Sylgar, its pet goldfish. Xanathar's attachment to the fish is total and terrifying. The Guild's agents who let anything bad happen to Sylgar can expect to be eaten. This is not a metaphor.

Guild field agent, currently held prisoner in the Zhentarim Interrogation House. Wears a leather skullcap stitched with fake beholder eyestalks. If rescued and cultivated, he can lead the PCs toward Xanathar's Lair. See the **Ott Steeltoes** page in Notable Figures.

campaign/setting/organizations/08-xanathars-guild.md:41: Xanathar's Guild is Waterdeep's largest criminal syndicate, run by a paranoid beholder from a fortified lair below the city. The Guild controls access to Skullport's smuggling networks, gladiatorial circuits, and a web of informants embedded throughout the wards. Its field operations are coordinated by Ahmaergo, and its three city outposts — a healer's clinic, a black market armorer, and a sweetshop front — form the chain that leads investigators toward the lair.

campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:8: > - This is the villain profile for Xanathar, the beholder crime lord of **Xanathar's Guild**. It sets out its three personality phases, its relationships, its goals and the one design note that makes it playable.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:9: > - Use it when you run any scene where Xanathar or the Guild acts or reacts. Phase 1 covers Acts I and II, Phase 2 covers Act III, and Phase 3 begins when the party enters its lair.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:11: > - The Guild's response teams, outposts, escalation row and revelation list live in the **Xanathar's Guild** page of the Factions Guide. Who the Guild is lives in the **Xanathar's Guild** page of Organizations.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:17: Xanathar is a beholder and the crime lord of **Xanathar's Guild**, a paranoid intelligence that has built a criminal empire around a terror of being vulnerable and wears the trappings of a monster because the trappings are convenient. Its core motive is to prove its supremacy and remove every threat to it, which means taking the Vault of Dragons, destroying Manshoon and keeping Skullport's money flowing.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:22: - The Guild keeps continuity by systematically eliminating anyone who knows more than the current Xanathar. After a century of this, the Guild is a surveillance network with no institutional memory beyond what the current beholder chooses to believe.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:24: - The current Xanathar believes it is the original. This belief is now structural to the Guild's identity rather than correctable. How the belief arose is not settled in any source, and the user has settled it ("Remix overtakes original campaign"): the remix line wins over the published adventure's area X33, where Xanathar gazes somberly at the tombs of its predecessors.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:25: - Skullport is the Guild's undercity foundation: the shadow city beneath Waterdeep, outside city jurisdiction, processing everything the surface economy won't handle. The Guild's relationship to Skullport is the relationship between a city and its sewers.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:27: - The Dock Ward is the Guild's primary surface territory.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:28: - Where the history leaves the Guild: an empire built on fear, run by a paranoid intelligence, served by people who survive by hiding their ambitions behind obedience.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:39: - Phase 1 → 2: PCs steal an Eye, raid a Guild location, or free a significant prisoner
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:40: - Phase 2 → 3: PCs enter the Lair, or multiple Guild operations fail in a short window

campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:56: - Nihiloor has been psionically dominating Xanathar for four months, so some decisions attributed to Xanathar are Nihiloor's (**Xanathar's Lair** structure notes, and the **Xanathar's Guild** organization page).
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:63: - **Eliminate Manshoon.** The splinter cell incursion is a personal insult. Method: Guild response teams targeting Zhentarim operations. There is no contingency, and this failure is part of what drives Phase 3.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:64: - **Maintain the Undercity.** Skullport supply lines fund the Guild. Method: Ahmaergo manages this operationally. Connects to Mad Mage integration.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:72: Xanathar's Guild is the largest criminal organization in Waterdeep, and the only one run by a beholder. Most Waterdhavians know it by rumor, as a smuggling trade through Skullport and a web of informants in every ward, with the Dock Ward as its main territory above ground. Its master, Xanathar, is said to rule from a fortified lair deep beneath the streets and to trust nobody who works for it. It is also known for an obsessive fondness for a goldfish named Sylgar.
campaign/setting/villains/xanathar.md:74: **Tone:** Plain and procedural for the GM tables, and grave in the Overview, where the Guild is a frightening local fact. Treat Xanathar's paranoia as dangerous and the goldfish as funny without letting the narration make the joke.

The wiki says the title was held by the second and later leaders of Xanathar's Thieves' Guild and derives from the personal name of its founder, "Xanathar (original)", listed under 1357 DR.

campaign/setting/waterdeep-lore.md:19: - **Dock Ward** (port, crime, contraband): the most dangerous ward; Xanathar's Guild's primary surface territory; Istrid Horn's moneylending operation works out of a warehouse here; the Sea Maidens Faire's carnival fleet moors here from Ches 21 for Fleetswake
campaign/setting/waterdeep-lore.md:53: **Tone:** Dense, specific, practical. Every fact should imply a consequence. The dragonward matters because Jarlaxle moves by sea. Guild membership matters because Trollskull Manor needs permits. The Watch matters because faction violence eventually reaches it.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:23: Three villain factions are visible inside the Yawning Portal before Volo Geddarm sits down to make his offer. Yagra Stonefist, a Doom Raiders operative and Davil Starsong's bodyguard, picks a fight with a Xanathar Guild thug named Krentz over a killing two nights ago in the Dock Ward, the same killing that lit the fuse on the ward's gang war. Four of Krentz's companions pile in. Before Durnan of the Yawning Portal can handle it himself, the Undermountain bell rings. A troll hauls itself out of the well with six stirges attached. Durnan vaults the bar, greatsword in hand, tells the party to deal with the stirges, and takes the troll. At the back of the settling taproom, seated near the observation balcony with a full glass of wine she has not touched, a drow woman watches the well. She has been at that table for two weeks. Volo arrives when the monster is dead, steered to the party's table by the contact who has been waiting for him, and describes a missing companion: Floon Blagmaar, wavy red-blond hair, early thirties, gone for two nights.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:27: Manshoon's Zhentarim hold the warehouse. Five of Urstul Floxin's thugs grabbed Renaer Neverember and Floon outside the Old Xoblob Shop two nights ago, brought them here, and questioned Renaer about his father's embezzled hoard. They took his mourning locket. Renaer believed it held a keepsake from his mother; it hid an Eye of the Stone of Golorr in a sealed compartment. The Eye was delivered to Kolat Towers before the Xanathar Guild raid killed most of the cell. Four kenku now search the wreckage. Renaer hid when the raid hit and is still hiding in a back room. Floon was mistaken for Renaer and dragged through a trapdoor into the sewer. Renaer, once found, tells the party what he saw: Xanathar agents, circle-eye tattoos, Floon unconscious over one man's shoulder, the trapdoor in the cellar floor. Three more clues in the warehouse confirm the route. They follow.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:29: In the sewers, Xanathar's yellow-chalk eye markers guide the way to a converted brick vault, nine rooms of a decommissioned filtration chamber, now a Xanathar Guild cell. Floon is alive in a holding room, bruised and grateful, and his first question is about Renaer. The cell's leader carries keys to every room, including the sealed iron door at the back of the vault. Behind that door, in a chamber they were not meant to reach, something withdraws before they get a clear look: a rounded silhouette trailing three or four thin tendrils, retreating into a floor grate. Any character who approached felt it first — a single pulse of cold, alien patience pressed against their mind. The grate descends into Undermountain. They do not follow. Floon is rescued. The party surfaces. Volo, embarrassed and grateful, signs over the deed to a derelict tavern in Trollskull Alley. It is called Trollskull Manor. It has not served a drink in months. It is now theirs.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:43: *The Bar Brawl.* Within fifteen minutes of the party's arrival, the brawl starts. Yagra Stonefist (see Appendix A), a Doom Raiders operative and Davil Starsong's bodyguard, has been nursing a drink at the bar while watching a table of five Xanathar Guild thugs led by Krentz — a man with eye-shaped tattoos shaved into his scalp. Two nights ago, Krentz's crew killed a Doom Raiders operative in the Dock Ward. One of the Xanathar thugs says something audible across the room about snake men getting what they deserved. Yagra crosses the room. Before she throws the first punch, she says: "You had no quarrel with us. Those weren't your turf. That was my friend." Krentz: "Your friend." Pause. "He's dead." First punch.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:77: *Faction Visibility.* Doom Raiders: Yagra, flying snake tattoo on her neck, Zhentarim pendant worn openly. Xanathar Guild: Krentz and four thugs with eye-shaped tattoos on shaved heads. Bregan D'Aerthe: the silent drow observer at the balcony table. Manshoon's Zhentarim: not present at the Yawning Portal; the Dock Ward killing Yagra referenced is their work.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:89: *Dock Ward Environment.* Streets are dark — most streetlamps smashed, candles stolen. Salt air and something worse. Tall tenements press together until midday light barely reaches the cobblestones. Yellow chalk eye-symbols on a few alley walls for those who know them (DC 15 Perception; finding them does not advance the investigation but tells a character familiar with the Xanathar Guild that this is their territory).
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:94: - **Old Xoblob Shop.** A deep gnome spy for the Xanathar Guild, Xoblob witnessed the attack outside his shop. He is reluctant to talk; a bribe of any purple item or a DC 13 Charisma (Intimidation or Persuasion) check loosens him. He describes five men in black leather armor grabbing Floon and a well-dressed companion outside his door; one had a winged snake tattoo on his neck. He also notes that one of the attackers grabbed the companion's silver necklace — pendant shaped like a horse, or possibly a unicorn. The stuffed beholder in Xoblob's window is a magical sensor through which Xanathar occasionally views the street; this is not discoverable at Arc A level.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:109: *Faction Visibility.* Xanathar Guild: yellow chalk territory marks (passive). Manshoon's Splinter Zhentarim: under arrest at the crime scene, or at the Skewered Dragon, or approaching to intimidate. Doom Raiders: not present in the Dock Ward; the bar brawl at the Yawning Portal established the faction fracture.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:125: - **Z1 — Main Room.** Tables and chairs overturned. Twelve corpses along the walls: five Zhentarim thugs (flying snake tattoos on neck or forearm) and seven Xanathar Guild thugs (one has a circle with ten spokes on their right palm — Xanathar's mark). Rapiers and daggers lying nearby. Crates of weapons, rations, boots, and black uniforms. Four kenku are here unless they heard the party enter, in which case they hide behind overturned furniture. Kenku speak only in mimicked voices; DC 10 Charisma (Intimidation) to force useful repetition — captured kenku say, in three different voices: "Xanathar sends its regards" (deep, orcish-accented); "Tie up the pretty boy in the back room!" and "Follow the yellow signs in the sewers" (thin, nasally); "No time to loot the place. Just get him to the boss" (scratchy). Characters attempting to enter without announcing themselves make a group DC 13 Dexterity (Stealth) check against the kenku's passive Perception of 13. If the kenku heard noise before entry (forced door, combat in the yard), the DC rises to 16.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:131: *Finding Renaer.* Renaer (see Appendix A) speaks freely once the kenku are dealt with, asking only for confirmation that the building is clear before emerging from Z2. He explains: five Zhentarim grabbed him and Floon outside Old Xoblob Shop last night. They brought him here and questioned him about his father's embezzled hoard — money Renaer refuses to help his father hide. They took his mourning locket, which he believed held a keepsake from his mother; he did not know there was a compartment inside. The Xanathar Guild attacked before the interrogation went further. He slipped his bonds in the confusion and hid. From a gap in the Z2 door he watched Xanathar agents drag an unconscious Floon — the man they mistook for Renaer — through the main room, out through the yard, and down the alley toward a sewer grate.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:137: *Three Independent Clue Paths to Revelation #3 (Floon is in the Xanathar Guild sewer hideout):*
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:147: ### Scene 4: The Xanathar Guild Sewer Hideout
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:155: Midway through the route, at a branching tunnel intersection, a **gazer guard** — a small beholder-kin under the Guild cell's command — watches the junction. It is hostile and cannot be negotiated with. A party moving carefully (group DC 13 Dexterity [Stealth] check) may neutralize or bypass it without alerting the hideout. A party that defeats it in open combat should assume the noise carries to Q1 — the kenku sentries are now listening. The gazer's presence is one of the WDH source's two signals that Xanathar's surveillance network extends into the sewers; defeating or bypassing it opens the correct passage, which leads to the hideout in five minutes following the guildsign.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:157: *Location Overview.* An eleven-room Xanathar Guild cell built into a decommissioned sewer vault beneath the Dock Ward. The complex runs from Q1 (entry, eastern approach) through Q11 (stone portal, western end). No natural light; the goblins in Q2 rely on darkvision. All interior doors are unlocked — Grum'shar assumes only those with guild business will reach this place. The Q7 boss chamber holds Floon and is where Nihiloor makes its appearance; Q11 is the escape portal Nihiloor uses to vanish into Xanathar's lair.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:173: *Nihiloor.* Nihiloor (see Appendix E) arrives at Q7 before the party, using Grum'shar's interrogation session as cover to observe Floon. The party's encounter with it is designed to be a sight-line, not a fight: they see it rise, see it release the intellect devourer, and watch it glide purposefully out of the room. It does not attack the party directly — it uses dominate monster only to clear its escape path if blocked. The goal of this moment is not horror; it is scale. The party glimpses what is actually running the Xanathar Guild cell and understands immediately that Grum'shar was never the top of this structure. Nihiloor's full scope surfaces across Arcs B through F.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:194: | 1 → 2 | A | The party rescues Floon Blagmaar from the Xanathar Guild sewer hideout | 200 |
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:212: ***The Two Zhentarims in Arc A.*** The original adventure presents the Zhentarim as a single faction. This remix uses Arc A to establish the faction fracture that defines the Manshoon subplot. Yagra Stonefist — a Doom Raiders operative, Davil Starsong's people — is at the Yawning Portal bar, openly wearing the Zhentarim pendant, fighting Xanathar Guild thugs. The Candle Lane warehouse belongs to Manshoon's cell, Urstul Floxin's people, operating separately from Davil. Players who later join the Doom Raiders in Arc B and then raid Kolat Towers in Arc I will have seen both Zhentarim factions from Scene 1 — one as a potential ally, one as an adversary, both operating under the same symbol. The distinction should be felt before it is explained.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:218: ***Nihiloor as Foreshadowing.*** Q9 in the sewer hideout is the campaign's first contact with Nihiloor, who is eventually revealed in Arc F to be the true intelligence driving the Xanathar Guild — running three parallel projects, exercising psionic control over Xanathar, and infiltrating Waterdeep's civil institutions (see Appendix E). In Arc A, Nihiloor does not engage with the party. A telepathic pulse and a silhouette in the dark are the full extent of the encounter. This restraint is intentional. Players who investigate Q9 should feel that they glimpsed something vast and wrong that they could not reach. Nihiloor's full scope surfaces across Arcs B through F. By the time the party reaches Undermountain Level 17 in the Dungeon of the Mad Mage, the creature's presence in Arc A should read as a thread that began in the sewers under Trollskull Alley.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:235: 4. **Which Zhentarim cell at the warehouse.** Manshoon's cell — Urstul Floxin's thugs. Not the Doom Raiders. The Eye extracted from Renaer's locket was delivered to Kolat Towers before the Xanathar Guild raid.

campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:25: While the renovation runs, Waterdeep comes to them. Guild inspectors arrive with clipboards and expectations. Neighbors appear with gifts, concerns, and agendas. Each of the city's seven adventuring factions sends its own recruiter, targeting specific party members by alignment, race, and reputation, not the group as a whole. The same party might find a Harper paper bird on one windowsill, a Bregan D'aerthe surveillance trail developing behind a drow PC, and a Doom Raiders flying snake delivering a note to the party's most morally flexible member, all in the same week. The factions want specific people. The party has to decide what they want in return.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:57: ### Scene 2: The Guild Gauntlet
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:63: *The Permit Checklist.* Broxley Fairkettle (Fellowship of Innkeepers) has given the party a list. Hammond Kraddoc (Vintners', Distillers', and Brewers' Guild) showed up uninvited with a second list and his scribe Jinny. Between them, the party has a map of required visits: Carpenters', Roofers', and Plaisterers' Guild (structural work); Cellarers' and Plumbers' Guild (basement and plumbing); Guild of Butchers (meat supply); Bakers' Guild (bread and pastries); Launderers' Guild (linens); Watchful Order of Magists and Protectors (fire suppression ward). Each requires a site inspection and a fee. Cross-ref **Appendix F, Part 1** for the guild fee table; this scene presents the social encounters, not the spreadsheet.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:67: *The Cellarers' and Plumbers' Guild (New Content).* The inspector finds the sewer access grate at the alley's east end (T7) and connects it to the manor's cellar through old city infrastructure — a direct link to Waterdeep's sewer network that predates the current building by at least two centuries. He flags this for "structural heritage documentation," which is not a blocking condition but must be noted. **This passage becomes relevant when faction response teams use the sewer approach, and when the party re-enters the sewer network in Arc C.**
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:69: *The Carpenters' Guild Negotiation (New Content).* The foreman's quoted renovation timeline is 18 days. A DC 14 Charisma (Persuasion) check or 20 gp above the standard fee reduces it to 12 days. A character with the Guild Artisan background or proficiency in Artisan's Tools reduces the DC by 2. The party's first negotiation in a city where knowing what you're buying matters more than the price itself.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:71: *Other Guilds.* Brief beats: Justyn Rassk (Guild of Butchers) delivers meat, demands a 3 gp gratuity above guild fees, and implies unpleasant things about future deliveries if he doesn't get it. This is a bluff — he has no capacity to follow through — but reading it requires a DC 13 Wisdom (Insight) check. Kraddoc appears at the Vintners' Guild inspection, chastises the party's current beverage selection, and suggests specific guild products "the guild is emphasizing this tenday." Buying two of them reduces the first month's supply rate by 5 gp. He will never acknowledge this as a social visit. Cross-ref **Appendix A** (Broxley Fairkettle profile, Hammond Kraddoc profile, Justyn Rassk note).
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:73: *Emmek Frewn's First Move.* During or immediately after the guild visits, Frewn begins his interference campaign. These conversations don't block any permit but impose a −2 penalty on guild inspection DCs for the first week. More visibly: Shard Shunner wererats in halfling form are casing Trollskull Manor as potential staff applicants, looking for entry points. **Cross-ref Appendix A** (Emmek Frewn profile) for his full character detail. campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:92: - **Speak with Dead — The Two Dead Zhentarim.** Bashekk Ortallis and Wern Malkrave each provide up to five answers. campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:106: *Faction Response Teams Deploy.* All four villain factions move on the crime scene within hours. campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:255: - 1+ days after death: team is briefed on Dalakhar's death; knows Floxin was following him; aware the Guild now has eyes on Floxin; can inadvertently provide the Gralhund connection (track Floxin → Yellowspire safe house → Gralhund Villa).
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:259: - **Mirt (Harpers, Renown 1+):** Identifies the mark without hesitation. Shares a rough map of observed Guild patrol patterns in the Trades Ward. Notes that Harper intelligence may be tainted — Manshoon has placed double agents in the network.

campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:262: - **Davil Starsong (Doom Raiders, Renown 1+):** Examines the mark with professional detachment. Confirms Guild death marks have been increasing. He is calculating whether the same leak that burned Dalakhar is going to burn him next — and whether Floxin filed the anonymous Watch tip naming the Doom Raiders as persons of interest, drawing heat away from Manshoon's cell.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:275: - Correspondence with Hammond Kraddoc of the Vintners', Distillers', and Brewers' Guild confirming large bribes paid to cover a contaminated-liquor scandal in the Dock Ward.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:360: - **Appendix E: Villain Factions** — Faction response team rosters and deployment behavior; Xanathar Guild gazer network; Manshoon parallel investigation; Bregan D'Aerthe conditional activation scaling with party choices at the Faire.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:386: ***The First Arc Where All Four Villain Factions Pursue the Same Object.*** Arc A establishes the Xanathar Guild and Manshoon's Zhentarim as adversaries. Arc B introduces the Cassalanters as patrons and Bregan D'Aerthe as observers. Arc C is the first arc where all four factions reach for the same object at the same time in the same city. The design makes this concrete rather than atmospheric: Xanathar gazers in the alley, Zhent parallel investigation, BD conditional observers scaling with party choices at the Faire, Cassalanter condolence note and dinner invitation. These are not flavor. They are the Grand Game becoming visible for the first time — the moment the party understands they are not the only investigators in Waterdeep, and that the city is full of people who already know more than they do.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:300: - **Response Team intercept:** If the DM wants a harder version of this scene, a Xanathar Guild response team (pre-positioned in the Dock Ward) cuts off the rooftop exit route, producing a three-way confrontation for the Stone. Cross-ref Appendix E response team rosters.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:339: - **Lords' Alliance (Jalester):** His debrief request arrives within twenty-four hours. The Watch officers on the Saerdoun Street beat were transferred to a Far South Ward assignment the week before the raid — a reassignment he has traced to a request from inside the Watch hierarchy, source unknown. He wants to know what the party saw inside the villa, and whether there was any evidence of Watch coordination with the Xanathar Guild or the Gralhund household.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:340: - **Emerald Enclave (Melannor):** Gralhund Villa sits over a minor ley line the Enclave monitors as part of Jeryth's ongoing aberrant-activity survey. The Xanathar Guild's presence in the neighborhood destabilized it over the past week in a way that correlates with Nihiloor's movements below the city. Melannor wants to know what happened inside. Not for political reasons. For the map.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:342: - **Force Grey (Vajra):** Vajra wants a full operational debrief — which factions were present, where each one entered and exited, whether any Watch checkpoint in the surrounding blocks was compromised or unmanned during the raid, and any direct evidence of Nihiloor's involvement. Xanathar Guild activity in close proximity to a Watch checkpoint the Guild has never previously approached is, in her judgment, a test. She wants to know if the test succeeded.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:423: - **Appendix E: Villain Factions** — Manshoon's Weave motivation and blackmailed Masked Lords (Corylus Thann, Jelenn Urmbrusk); *A Directive from Jarlaxle* document listing; *Gralhund Study of the Grand Game* document listing; Yellowspire section (Chirada, Second Eye, teleportal); Xanathar Guild response teams (Scene 5 gazer intercept option).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:15: Ten outposts are distributed across Waterdeep's wards, maintained by four factions, each one a data point in the web of the Grand Game. The party cannot storm the lairs blind — Xanathar's sanctum, Cassalanter Villa's temple, the Sea Maidens Faire, and Kolat Towers are not targets until the party knows enough to attempt them. The outposts are the research: entering a Xanatharian front to find how the Guild accesses its own lair, or breaking into a cultist's apartment to find a map the Cassalanters spent months assembling.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:39: The Stone now sits with the party. The Watch crackdown following the Gralhund Villa incident has disrupted Manshoon's Brindul Alley operation, scattered the Zhentarim response teams, and landed Davil Starsong in Watch custody on circumstantial evidence. Xanathar's Guild is consolidating — pulling agents tighter, increasing surveillance. Jarlaxle watches the party with quiet interest and no urgency; he holds something they need, and he is in no hurry to discuss terms. The Cassalanters, undisturbed, continue their research. Below the city's peaceful surface, four factions know the Stone has surfaced. Three of them want it back.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:81: *Mirt (Harpers — renown 1+).* The Harpers have been watching the Cassalanter family for years, and Mirt's summary reflects sustained, inconclusive surveillance. The Cassalanters give more to charity than houses twice their wealth, never miss a temple service, and have not made a single political enemy in a decade. "Nobody is that clean," Mirt says, without pleasure. He does not know what they are hiding. The Harpers have found no evidence of wrongdoing — only the complete absence of any wrongdoing at all, which in Waterdeep's political ecology is itself suspicious. He recommends starting with Xanathar outposts on the grounds that the Guild's paranoia makes early strikes more effective than late ones. His unstated reason: the Harpers have already exhausted their Xanathar intelligence, and the Cassalanter question is the one he cannot answer and cannot let go.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:115: **Xanathar's Guild:** Grinda Garloth's operates openly in Mistshore as a black-market armorer. Anyone asking about Xanathar weapons or Guild suppliers in the Dock Ward gets pointed to her within a day. No credentials required.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:135: ### Scene 4: Xanathar's Guild Outposts
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:149: *Step 2 — Case the Target.* A modest, unremarkable rowhouse. Two kinds of visitors come: those seeking healing (legitimate front), and those attending gladiatorial events in Xanathar's Lair. Two passphrases control entry: "I heard the red hand was hot" accesses the healing operation; "the bawdy star shines bright tonight" gets a guest past the front rooms. See the **Xanathar's Guild** Factions guide page for the full area layout.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:153: *Step 4 — The Operation.* Primary objectives: recover the *Instructions for Gladiatorial Weaponry* from Keln's desk (Area 6–7), which names Grinda Garloth as the supplier and Ahmaergo as the authorizing agent; recover the *Injury Reports* logbook (Area 5 Upper Landing), which contains an entry noting a wound caused by "incautious observation of the windmill on Coachlamp Lane" — a cross-faction clue pointing toward the Cassalanter outpost chain; recover the *Ill Man's Note* (Area 8–10 Sick Rooms), which contains the Orb Confectioners passphrase in verse. Optional: follow a guest being transported to the gladiatorial event. The trap door in the kitchen leads to an underground dock; guests are blindfolded before descent and transported by boat to Area X4 of Xanathar's Lair. This route is a genuine lair entry method, but arriving via gladiatorial transport means arriving unprepared, blindfolded, and unarmed. See the **Xanathar's Guild** Factions guide page for keyed area details.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:165: *Approach.* Grinda operates openly as a black-market armorer. Anyone asking about Xanathar weapons, Guild suppliers, or black-market arms in the Dock Ward gets pointed to Mistshore within a day (DC 12 Charisma in Dock Ward taverns or street contacts; no faction membership required). Three additional paths: the *Instructions for Gladiatorial Weaponry* from Terasse Estate names her; a captured Xanathar response team member's *Directive from Nar'l* names her as a supplier; Agorn Fuoco's *Report to Agorn Fuoco* (from any Zhentarim response team) confirms her location. Parties can approach as customers, investigators, or raiders. Grinda does not fight for the Guild — she fights for her business. She will negotiate if the party offers more immediate value than Xanathar does, and she will report the encounter afterward.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:167: *Key Areas.* Area D2: 500 gp and the *Note Regarding Gladiatorial Weaponry* from Ahmaergo, confirming delivery to Terasse Estate and citing Xanathar's expectation of "exotic gutting." Area D3: mundane arms and armor including single-shot pistols; 1d4 pieces of magic arms or armor (DMG Table F); 1d4 miscellaneous magic items (DMG Table A or B). See the **Xanathar's Guild** Factions guide page for full area descriptions.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:169: *Intel Yield.* The Ahmaergo correspondence confirms the Terasse connection and establishes Ahmaergo as a named Guild lieutenant. Grinda's knowledge of sewer patrol routes is available through negotiation or by locating her operational logbook — partial maps of Xanathar's Lair approach corridors. The magic item inventory is genuine loot.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:181: *Approach.* Three paths to Orb Confectioners: a captured Xanathar response team member carries the passphrase or the location in their directive; Agorn Fuoco's *Report to Agorn Fuoco* names Orb Confectioners on Rising Ride; Terasse Estate's *Ill Man's Note* contains the passphrase in verse. The passphrase exchange: ask for "eight small eyes." The counter-staff responds: "How would you like those arranged?" Answer: "Around an ocular cake." Correct answers earn an escort to the cellar stairs. Use the Autumn Cellar Complex layout (DH p. 86). See the **Xanathar's Guild** organization page.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:183: *Key Areas.* Area B7: *On the Delivery of a Mechanical Beholder* from Ahmaergo, setting a delivery deadline of Tarsakh 9th for a mechanical beholder to Terasse Estate — confirms the gladiatorial supply chain and ties Ahmaergo to Xanathar's engineering operations. Area B9b: a **mimic** disguised as the chest at the foot of Korgstrod's bed. A hidden coffer under the bed (DC 13 to find) holds 500 gp and *Correspondence with Nar'l Xibrindas*: Nar'l's letter references smokepowder and provides guildsign directions toward Area X1 of Xanathar's Lair. The guildsign trail requires DC 15 checks, rising to DC 17 near the lair itself; three failures before a success means the trail is lost. See the **Xanathar's Guild** organization page.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:209: *Ott Steeltoes.* A gnome prisoner wearing a leather skullcap stitched with fake beholder eyestalks — an Xanatharian agent captured during the Guild war. Ott does not know what the Stone of Golorr is, but he knows Xanathar's Lair. If rescued and cultivated, he offers to introduce the party to the Guild or guide them personally to the lair's entrance. If freed without follow-through, he disappears back into Xanathar's network. See the **Ott Steeltoes** page in Notable Figures and the **Xanathar's Guild** organization page.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:11: The lair beneath Skullport has been sealed for weeks. Since the fireball disrupted the Street of Silver trade routes, Xanathar has withdrawn into paranoia — convinced that one of the other Grand Game factions has mapped the lair's entrances. Ahmaergo runs external operations while Xanathar broods in the Sanctum, running its eye rays across the empty room and interrogating Guild members whose faces it does not immediately recognize. The gladiatorial games continue because the arena revenue continues, and because stopping them would signal weakness. But Xanathar does not attend from its throne as it once did. It watches from invisibility, alone.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:13: Nihiloor has used this paranoia as cover. The illithid's three unauthorized projects — running in parallel for years — are now approaching critical phases. The Infiltration: intellect devourers inserted into four positions in Waterdeep's Watch and civic administration, with two additional placements pending. The Experiments: twelve captives from the gladiatorial arena modified into psionic relay organisms, intended to form the backbone of a city-wide intelligence network that would answer only to Nihiloor. The Puppet: four months ago, Nihiloor successfully applied Dominate Monster to Xanathar through a sustained psionic relay device in X26. The Xanathar who has been running the Guild since then — the Xanathar who killed Manshoon's envoys, who sent response teams against the party's allies in **Faction Outposts**, who holds the Eye in a fishbowl because it trusts Ott's aesthetic judgment — has been Nihiloor's instrument. The beholder does not know this. The domination does not feel like domination; it feels like its own decisions.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:17: Jarlaxle placed Nar'l Xibrindas in the Guild in 1491 and has relied on him as a double agent for the year since. Through Nar'l's monthly reports, the Bregan D'aerthe have assembled a precise picture of the lair's layout — including the secret passage at X14 that bypasses the panopticus entirely and opens forty feet from the Sanctum door. Jarlaxle has been waiting for an opportunity to retrieve the Stone and its Eye. He has been in no hurry. He has always been certain the opportunity would come.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:31: *Mirt (Harpers — renown 1+).* Thorvin Twinbeard is inside the lair — a Harper asset embedded in the lair's engineering staff for six years, Mirt's most carefully kept secret. Mirt provides Thorvin's location (X13, the workshop) and a recognition code: ask him what he thinks of the new pulley design. His response — "It'll hold if the load is distributed right" — confirms the channel. If Xanathar outposts were hit in **Faction Outposts**, Mirt warns that Thorvin's cover may be compromised: a gazer has been assigned to watch him, and Thorvin knows it. At renown 10+, Mirt arranges a Harper field agent — use the spy stat block — to stage a timed surface distraction above the guildsign entrance: a staged brawl that occupies Guild topside watchers for 20 minutes at a prearranged signal.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:41: *Tashlyn Yafeera or Davil Starsong (Doom Raiders — renown 1+).* Contact depends on quest order: Tashlyn if **DR Mission 4 — Silencing Skeemo** is not yet complete (Davil still in custody), Davil if it has been (he's back at the Yawning Portal). Either way, the intelligence is the same — secondhand accounts from Guild agents who defected to the Doom Raiders over the past two years. The Noska–Ahmaergo rivalry has reached near-blows twice in the past month; during an active tournament, Noska is arena-side and Ahmaergo is in his quarters reviewing fight records, neither monitoring the other's territory. That window is real and exploitable. At renown 10+, Yagra Stonefist is available as outside backup — she can hold the guildsign entrance open from the exterior, delay a pursuing Guild patrol by 5 minutes, or cover the party's extraction from the sewer exit. Once per operation.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:63: **Ott Steeltoes as Guide (if rescued from the Interrogation House).** Ott knows the lair's maintenance corridors — service tunnels the Guild's rank-and-file barely know exist. He guides the party through sewer access to a service entrance at X15, providing guard schedule gaps and specific patrol timings. Advantage: the guided approach bypasses both X2 and X4 checkpoints entirely; the party enters through infrastructure, not a guarded threshold. Disadvantage: Ott is terrified. Under pressure, make a DC 12 Wisdom saving throw for Ott at the start of each combat — on a failure, he freezes, stammers loud enough to draw attention, or attempts to flee. If captured, he tells the Guild everything within minutes.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:79: *Safe Zone: Area X28.* The Guild Recreation Hall has no panopticus sensor. Nihiloor built this blind spot intentionally; rank-and-file Guild members speak more freely without surveillance, and their unguarded conversations are useful intelligence. The party can use X28 as a staging area once they reach it: rest, plan, treat wounds, or establish a rendezvous. Guild members here are occupied with card games and arguments and will not scrutinize strangers with a plausible story.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:95: If the gazer is destroyed (13 HP, AC 13), Thorvin speaks openly for the first time. His relief is immediate and genuine. He confirms what the party may have begun to suspect: Nihiloor's domain (X23–X27) contains evidence that would change everything the party thinks they know about who has been running Xanathar's Guild.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:101: Without BD allegiance revealed: Nar'l is coolly professional. He offers mild operational assistance framed as Guild hospitality, pointing the party toward X28 as a rest point, confirming which guards accept bribes and at what rate. He reveals nothing about himself.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:111: Consequences: Xanathar is destroyed if present in the lair, or escapes through the Undermountain connection at X4 if the GM wants to preserve the thread. The Guild collapses into factional fighting between Ahmaergo and surviving lieutenants. Nar'l and Thorvin must be evacuated or they die. Thorvin will not leave the lair's surviving workers; Nar'l will leave without hesitation. Nihiloor was not in his domain at the time and escapes to Undermountain Level 17 with his three projects intact.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:113: Permanent consequences: the Guild is gone as an intelligence resource. The **Vault of Dragons** approach loses access to Guild knowledge and contacts. The Undermountain connection from X4 is sealed. The party trades a dangerous faction they can understand for an unconstrained Nihiloor operating below the city with no leash.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:141: **X2 — Entrance Hall.** A vaulted stone chamber with beholder motifs carved into every pillar. Two Guild Soldiers stand watch at the far end. The panopticus sensor is embedded in the ceiling above the entrance, angled to capture anyone passing through. Passive Perception 14 notices the crystal housing. At Suspicious tier: visitors must show a Guild pass (leaden disk, beholder eye stamp). At Alert and above: no visitors admitted without escort.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:147: **X7 — Cell Block.** Eight cells cut into rock, three occupied. Samara (gladiatorial combatant, six weeks in captivity) knows: Xanathar holds "a magic stone and a marble with a colored center"; the marble is in "the fish tank in the big room with the purple carpet"; and Manshoon's organization is based in a tower in the North Ward (overheard from another prisoner since sent to the arena). The tower information is one of three clue paths to **Kolat Towers**. Two Guild Soldiers watch the cell gate; a third is asleep in a chair. Cell locks are DC 13 Dexterity (Thieves' Tools). If all three prisoners are freed, the two Guild members in custody offer to start a fight at X2 — buying the party 5 minutes before being subdued.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:151: **X13 — Thorvin's Workshop.** Cluttered with mechanical components and active engineering projects. Thorvin works here during daylight hours. A gazer floats near the ceiling and watches him constantly. Thorvin communicates via encoded technical-sounding statements — "the third pulley from the north has a load problem" means the third guard station north of X1 is on elevated alert. The party extracts intelligence by asking about specific "mechanical issues." If the gazer is destroyed, Thorvin speaks openly, confirms Nihiloor's projects, and tells the party that the entity running the Guild is not the one making its decisions.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:155: **X18 — Audience Chamber.** The lair's largest upper-level room. A high-vaulted chamber with a raised dais; the panopticus sensor here is the widest-field in the lair, covering the entire chamber floor and both side corridors. During an active audience session: 15–25 Guild members waiting for their turn. The door to X19 is behind the dais — two guards flank it during empty periods; four guards flank it during an active session.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:165: **X28 — Guild Recreation Hall.** No scrying sensor. Card games, arm wrestling, complaints about Xanathar. Off-duty Guild members here will not scrutinize strangers with a plausible story. The party can rest up to 1 hour, plan next steps, or wait out a patrol sweep.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:167: **X33 — Crypt of Xanathar's Past.** Through the secret door from X6 (DC 17 Perception), a narrow passage leads to a chamber containing the preserved remains and personal effects of every previous beholder who held the title "Xanathar" — predecessors stretching back centuries. Nar'l Xibrindas is the only current Guild member who knows this room exists. The room contains: evidence that "Xanathar" is a title claimed through murder, not inherited; the original Guild charter signed by the first holder; and locked coffer containing the personal journals of the last three Xanathars, including early dealings with Manshoon's predecessors. The revelation that Xanathar is a title implies the current one could be replaced, and that someone may already be planning to do so.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:169: **X35 — Nar'l's Office.** Sparse and organized by a mind that trusts nothing to memory when paper will do. The desk holds Guild operational reports and a coded communication log in Jarlaxle's cipher (DC 16 Intelligence to decode without the key). In a false-bottomed drawer (DC 14 Intelligence (Investigation)): the *Report on the Seven Masks Theater* — a summary of Jarlaxle's operational activity through the theater's ownership chain, its connection to the Sea Maidens Faire, and a partial decoding of Jarlaxle's Grand Game position. Nar'l has kept this from Xanathar for eleven months. Finding it is one of three clue paths to **Sea Maidens Faire**.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:171: **X36 — Smokepowder Cache.** A sealed maintenance alcove marked on Thorvin's schematic as "decommissioned drainage junction." Eight smokepowder kegs wrapped in Guild-color cloth. DC 15 Intelligence (Investigation) without Thorvin's schematic; DC 11 with it. The clockwork detonators Thorvin provides take 20 minutes to assemble (DC 15 Dexterity, Thorvin's tools required) and trigger simultaneously via pull-cord from up to 100 feet.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:178: - **X26 — The Puppet.** A psionic relay device, currently active, maintaining a Dominate Monster effect on a psychic signature that matches Xanathar's. Detailed notes track behavioral corrections applied over four months: which tactical decisions were changed, which Guild members were elevated or removed at Nihiloor's direction, and the long-term strategic repositioning of the Guild toward Nihiloor's actual goals. The beholder who rules Xanathar's Guild does not rule Xanathar's Guild. Every Xanathar decision the party has encountered was Nihiloor's decision.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:186: Area-assigned: Guild Soldiers in pairs at X2, X4, X18 (doubled at Alert tier); Xanathar's Guards at X7, door detail at X18, and X19 during Xanathar's absence; Panopticus Dwarves (3) in X16, non-combatants.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:194: **Stage 1 — Local Alert.** Trigger: panopticus detects unauthorized movement (panopticus not suborned), a guard is attacked, or a Guild member shouts alarm. Response: X16 signals nearest guard station; two Guild Soldiers dispatched to the triggered area. Response time: 1d4+1 minutes.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:198: **Stage 3 — Full Alert.** Trigger: a named NPC (Ahmaergo, Nar'l, Noska) confirms combat with intruders. Response: all off-duty Guild members in X28 and X8 mobilize; Ahmaergo leads a team toward the reported position; Xanathar retreats to X19 with four Guild Soldiers. Response time: 1d6 minutes from Stage 2.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:280: **Xanathar's Guild:** Set the "Xanathar operational?" flag to one of three states:
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:284: - *GONE — Lair demolished or Xanathar killed:* Guild collapses into Ahmaergo-led factional fighting. Response teams disbanded. Nihiloor escapes to Undermountain Level 17. The party permanently loses Guild intelligence as a resource. **Vault of Dragons**'s approach routes through former Guild contacts who are now independent actors.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:286: **Manshoon's Zhentarim Splinter.** Monitoring via agents watching the lair's entrances. If Xanathar is weakened or eliminated, Manshoon moves within days to absorb abandoned Guild territory. His alert tier increases if the party was observed.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:295: - **Jalester:** "How did the Guild respond? Are their patrol routes disrupted?" Lords' Alliance interest: Guild operational status affects city security assessments.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:299: - **Tashlyn or Davil:** "Is Xanathar still operational?" Direct and tactical — the Doom Raiders' expansion into former Guild territory depends on the answer. "And Ahmaergo?"
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:341: - the **Xanathar's Guild** organization page — Xanathar's Guild faction overview and NPC roster. **Xanathar's Guild** Factions guide page — outpost entries, response team compositions, escalation framework, Revelation List, Nihiloor's three-project breakdown.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:400: The case for it is real: a villain faction eliminated, a dangerous location made safe, a complicated enemy relationship resolved with finality. The case against it is also real: permanent loss of Guild intelligence, Nihiloor freed from his puppet's leash and operating unconstrained from Undermountain, Nar'l and Thorvin killed unless the party explicitly evacuates them, the Undermountain connection severed before the campaign descends. The party that destroys the lair is trading a faction they understand — paranoid, predictable, with known structures and exploitable NPCs — for an unknown quantity in Nihiloor, who is smarter than Xanathar, does not have Xanathar's attachment to a goldfish, and has been running the Guild in secret for months without anyone noticing.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:406: Nihiloor's three projects have been written as discoverable, not required. The party completes the heist by reaching X19 and extracting the Eye — nothing in X23–X27 is needed for that. What the secondary objective zone offers is the campaign's deepest active conspiracy, available to parties thorough enough to explore a dangerous area that even the Guild's guards avoid.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:408: The Puppet revelation is the most significant: Xanathar has been Nihiloor's instrument for four months. Every aggressive, paranoid, apparently irrational decision attributed to the beholder in **Faction Outposts** and this quest was Nihiloor's decision. This changes the party's understanding retroactively. The mastermind they've been treating as the main threat was itself controlled by something worse. The real threat from Xanathar's Guild faction was never Xanathar.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:430: **Order of the Gauntlet.** Savra Belabranta considers the use of intellect devourers in Xanathar's Guild a specific theological offense — aberrant intelligence overwriting natural souls. She offers to accompany the party into Xanathar's lair as her Renown 3 companion benefit (once per quest), but only if the party commits to eliminating Nihiloor permanently rather than bypassing him. If that commitment cannot be made, she does not join. The offer is genuine; so is the condition. Nihiloor always escapes, so she asks for the attempt, and she stays with the party when he gets away. See the Quest Hooks section of the **Order of the Gauntlet** Factions guide page.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:45: *Tashlyn Yafeera or Davil Starsong (Doom Raiders — renown 1+).* The Cassalanters hold banking leverage over half the guilds in Waterdeep. Tashlyn or Davil can provide intelligence on the household's operations: servant rotation schedules, delivery windows, and which guild contractors have regular access to the villa grounds. The Cellarers' and Plumbers' Guild has records of the villa's basement construction — partial blueprints available through a Doom Raider contact. These blueprints show the wine cellar (C28), the false vault (C27), and the original underground construction that predates the villa. A hatch between C22 and C23 appears on the old plans — it was not originally concealed. The blueprints do not show C29 (later construction) or the temple interior. At renown 10+, Yagra Stonefist drives a Black Network carriage and serves as lookout outside the villa during the operation — maintaining contact through a code system (a kerchief in a window) and ready to run an immediate extraction through the Sea Ward's back streets.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:65: **Entry 2 — Guild Blueprints and Service Access.** Construction records from the Cellarers', Stonecutters', Carpenters', and Surveyors' guilds provide partial villa floor plans. The Surveyors' Guild has an old site map showing underground construction predating the villa — the temple's original footprint. Advantage: advance knowledge of layout, including the false vault trap at C27 and the hatch between C22 and C23. A Doom Raider contact can arrange guild contractor uniforms — delivery crew, maintenance staff, or chandlers — that grant access to the villa's service areas without a social invitation. Disadvantage: blueprints do not show C29 (later construction) or the temple interior. Contractor cover expires when the real crew arrives — typically a 2-hour window.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:215: Guild blueprints from Scene 2 show this room's dimensions, which are smaller than the vault-space implied by the basement's footprint — a clue that C27 is not the true vault.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:355: **Xanathar's Guild.** If **Xanathar's Lair** already completed, no change. If not, Xanathar's awareness of the party's activities depends on imp surveillance from C25 — if the villa was assaulted rather than infiltrated, reports of unusual activity near the Sea Ward reach the Guild's informant network.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:15: The Eye had reached him the night before Ches 1, through Bregan D'aerthe intelligence work — the same network that had placed Nar'l Xibrindas inside Xanathar's Guild a year earlier, in 1491. Jarlaxle understood the Eye's significance better than any other faction in the Grand Game, which is why he was not rushing. He would watch. He would learn who else was moving on the Stone. If the party pursuing the Stone proved competent, he would engage them on his own terms. If they did not, he would take the Eye himself when the moment was right.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:35: *Jalester Silvermane (Lords' Alliance — renown 1+).* The Sea Maidens Faire uses private docking arrangements that circumvent standard Watch inspection protocols — Jalester flagged this irregularity in **Fireball!**. He can provide the Faire's official docking permits, which list crew numbers that do not match observed headcounts. If BD outposts were hit, Jalester can arrange harbor patrols to be "elsewhere" during a specific window. He also notes that the Council of Musicians filed an objection to the Faire as a public entertainment, resolved through the Jesters' Guild — the Faire has legal protection that makes Watch intervention politically difficult. If the alliance path is available (party has faction contacts willing to sponsor Luskan's Alliance bid), Jalester is the most relevant contact: any political commitment regarding Luskan runs through the Alliance council, and he will want to be briefed before the meeting with Jarlaxle. At renown 10+, Jalester can arrange a formal harbor inspection by Watch authority — a 90-minute window when three Faire crew members must answer questions aboard the Heartbreaker, reducing the Eyecatcher's available response roster. Requires 24 hours' notice; usable once.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:43: *Tashlyn Yafeera or Davil Starsong (Doom Raiders — renown 1+).* The Doom Raiders have had dealings with Jarlaxle's operation — he approached them early in the campaign about intelligence sharing, which they declined. Tashlyn or Davil can provide the Faire's supply schedule: food deliveries from the Butchers' Guild on Ches 22, 26, and Tarsakh 2, 6; water cask deliveries from the Watermen's Guild every three days; exotic animal feed from a private supplier in the Field Ward. Each delivery requires a crew of 4–6 and takes 45 minutes. Delivery crews have unescorted access to the cargo holds. The Doom Raiders also note that the Faire's revenue does not match its operating costs: "They're spending more than they're earning. The carnival is subsidized by something else." At renown 10+, Yagra Stonefist accompanies the supply delivery infiltration — she knows enough Guild cant to bluff past a cargo inspection and can carry concealed equipment for the party without triggering search protocols.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:65: **Supply Delivery Infiltration (via Doom Raiders / guild contacts).** Guild delivery schedules from the Doom Raiders provide windows when cargo is being loaded onto the docked ships. The party disguises as delivery crew — Butchers' Guild, Watermen's Guild, or the exotic animal feed supplier. Advantage: access to cargo holds of the Heartbreaker and Hellraiser without boarding checks; delivery carts provide concealment for equipment; 45-minute window of unescorted access. Disadvantage: does not reach the Eyecatcher directly (deliveries go to the docked ships only; Eyecatcher supplies are ferried by skiff); cover story fails if the real delivery crew arrives simultaneously.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:280: **Xanathar's Guild.** If **Xanathar's Lair** already completed, no change. If not, Xanathar's awareness depends on whether Guild patrols in the Dock Ward observed harbor activity.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:424: **To Xanathar's Lair (if not yet completed).** The Letter from Nar'l Xibrindas (U3) confirms the BD mole in Xanathar's organization and mentions the Seven Masks Theater purchase. This is one of three independent paths to **Xanathar's Lair** — connecting the BD intelligence network to Xanathar's Guild and providing operational context for the approach.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:11: Lady Gondafrey, then a City Watch captain and a knight of Tyr, attempted to breach the towers in 1379 DR while investigating Duhlark's suspected dealings with Xanathar's Guild. The force field repelled her. The arcane lock on the front door defeated her thieves. She attempted a third entry through the bridge on the third floor and was captured. Duhlark offered her a choice: remain imprisoned, or swear never to speak of what she had seen. She refused the oath. She has been in the towers since 1379 DR — 113 years.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:19: The blackmail operation is what funds the Splinter's political reach. Corylus Thann, a Masked Lord, was photographed in a situation that would end his career; he supplies Guild intelligence in exchange for the ledger's continued privacy. Jelenn Urmbrusk accumulated significant debt to a Splinter front organization; his votes in the Council of Guildmasters follow Manshoon's preferences without his ever being asked directly. The ledger documenting these arrangements, and three others, lives in an arcane-locked drawer in Manshoon's study in E13. The identity list in the same drawer names Mirt, Thardouk Starbuckler, and Dorgar Adarbrent as Masked Lords — intelligence Manshoon has been accumulating for reasons that are not yet clear.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:247: **The Blackmail Ledger (E13 desk).** Delivering it to Laeral Silverhand through Mirt or Vajra collapses Manshoon's political position — Corylus Thann and Jelenn Urmbrusk are exposed, their influence over Watch patrol assignments and Guildmaster votes evaporates, and two of Manshoon's most valuable civic tools are neutralized regardless of whether Manshoon himself survived. The identity list on the back pages names five Masked Lords total: Corylus Thann, Jelenn Urmbrusk, Mirt, Thardouk Starbuckler, and Dorgar Adarbrent. Mirt will want a word with the party privately before the ledger goes to Laeral, not to suppress the names but to explain them: "I volunteered. The others didn't."

campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:196: | Xanathar's Guild | WOUNDED (Xanathar injured, absent) | Ahmaergo leading a handful of Guild agents — opportunistic rather than personal | Recover the Stone if possible; deny the gold to rivals; no escalation beyond what Ahmaergo judges recoverable |

campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:98: | Artisan | Strong | Guild system is everything in Waterdeep; knows the permit labyrinth firsthand |
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:219: The job was supposed to be clean. The Xanathar Guild was acquiring something from a rival organization; the character was hired to facilitate transit and paid well enough not to ask questions. What came out of there wasn't what they'd been told. What they left behind was considerably worse. The Guild got its item. The irregularities were absorbed into a larger accounting of that night, and the character's involvement was not officially noted.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:223: *Your character knows the following:* The Guild does not know, officially, that you exist. Ahmaergo knows. He made one indirect inquiry months after the event, to confirm you were still in Waterdeep and still capable. You confirmed both by answering.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:225: **The Holder: Ahmaergo (Xanathar's Guild)**
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:226: A compact, humorless dwarf who is in practical terms the operational brain of the Xanathar Guild. He doesn't have Xanathar's moods or its capacity for sudden violence, which makes him considerably more dangerous to deal with: he's consistent. His first formal request arrives as a logistics problem — something that requires someone unaffiliated, deniable, and competent. He won't call it debt repayment. Characters who complete the task will find the *Outstanding* column now reads *Current.* Characters who refuse will find the official records suddenly contain their name in a context that requires immediate explanation to the City Watch.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:260: Skeemo Weirdbottle maintains, out of a cramped and thoroughly unassuming shop in the Dock Ward, one of the most comprehensive indices of Waterdeep's criminal and semi-criminal activities that exists outside the Xanathar Guild's own records. He compiled it partly for business purposes and partly because he finds the city's power structures intrinsically interesting as a subject of study. He sold you something from it — access, a specific piece of information, a document that enabled something you needed to do — and conducted the transaction with professional discretion.
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:39: - **Dock Ward** (port, crime, contraband):** the most dangerous ward; Xanathar Guild's primary surface territory; Sea Maidens Faire moors here during Fleetswake
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:72: **Tone:** Dense, specific, practical. Every fact should imply a consequence. The dragonward matters because Jarlaxle moves by sea. Guild membership matters because Trollskull Manor needs permits. The Watch matters because faction violence eventually reaches it.

campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:381: 6. **Xanathar's goldfish:** The DM should establish Sylgar early — Arc A or Arc B, through NPC gossip, a Watch report about a Guild response to a perceived insult involving a fish, anything that makes the detail feel like world texture before it becomes plot.
